which reduces the number of contributing vertices and edges in the computation of
{}^{\rm alt}\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
.

For the operator
{}^{\rm extr}\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
, Eq. (
99
) can no longer be applied.
Therefore, the symmetry implied by (
104
) is not manifested. To reduce the complexity of the computation, the following strategy is proposed. Consider a rotation, denoted by
\mathfrak{r}
, about the axis
(1/\sqrt{2},1/\sqrt{2},0)
for
\pi
radians which exchanges the
x
- and
y
-axes, and flips the
z
-axis. We obtain

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\tilde{H}}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}(v;v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4};e_{x}^{+},e_{y}^{+},e_{z}^{+})\rangle=\langle\hat{\tilde{H}}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}(\mathfrak{r}(v);\mathfrak{r}(v_{1}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{2}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{3}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{4});\mathfrak{r}(e_{x}^{+}),\mathfrak{r}(e_{y}^{+}),\mathfrak{r}(e_{z}^{+}))\rangle

(105)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\tilde{H}}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}(v;\mathfrak{r}(v_{1}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{2}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{3}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{4});e_{y}^{+},e_{x}^{+},e_{z}^{-})\rangle=\langle\hat{\tilde{H}}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}(v;\mathfrak{r}(v_{1}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{2}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{3}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{4});e_{y}^{+},e_{x}^{+},e_{z}^{+})\rangle

in which we use the definition of
\hat{\tilde{H}}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
in (
81
), assume
\mathfrak{r}(v)=v
without loss of generality. Moreover, the first equality of the last equation is obtained by using Eq. (
69
), the second one, by the definition of
\mathfrak{r}
and, the last one, by Eq. (
99
).

Then consider the operator

\displaystyle\hat{F}(v;v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4};e_{x}^{+},e_{y}^{+},e_{z}^{+})

(106)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}(v;v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4};e_{x}^{+},e_{y}^{+},e_{z}^{+})+\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}(v;\mathfrak{r}(v_{3}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{4}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{1}),\mathfrak{r}(v_{2});e_{x}^{+},e_{y}^{+},e_{z}^{+})

According to (
105
),
\hat{F}
is

\displaystyle\hat{F}(v;v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4};e_{x}^{+},e_{y}^{+},e_{z}^{+})

(107)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathrm{tr}([h_{e_{y}^{+}},[\hat{Q}_{\mathfrak{r}(v_{1})}^{2k_{1}},\hat{H}_{E}^{(k_{2})}(\mathfrak{r}(v_{2}))]]h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}[h_{e_{x}^{+}},[\hat{Q}_{\mathfrak{r}(v_{3})}^{2k_{3}},\hat{H}_{E}^{(k_{4})}(\mathfrak{r}(v_{4}))]]h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}[h_{e_{z}^{+}},\hat{Q}_{v}^{2k_{5}}]h_{e_{z}^{+}}^{-1})

\displaystyle+\mathrm{tr}([h_{e_{x}^{+}},[\hat{Q}_{\mathfrak{r}(v_{3})}^{2k_{1}},\hat{H}_{E}^{(k_{2})}(\mathfrak{r}(v_{4}))]]h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}[h_{e_{y}^{+}},[\hat{Q}_{\mathfrak{r}(v_{1})}^{2k_{3}},\hat{H}_{E}^{(k_{4})}(\mathfrak{r}(v_{2}))]]h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}[h_{e_{z}^{+}},\hat{Q}_{v}^{2k_{5}}]h_{e_{z}^{+}}^{-1})

up to an overall factor.
For clarity, we will denote

\displaystyle\hat{X}\equiv

\displaystyle[h_{e_{x}^{+}},[\hat{Q}_{\mathfrak{r}(v_{3})}^{2k_{3}},\hat{H}_{E}^{(k_{4})}(\mathfrak{r}(v_{4}))]]h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}

(108)

\displaystyle\hat{Y}\equiv

\displaystyle[h_{e_{y}^{+}},[\hat{Q}_{\mathfrak{r}(v_{1})}^{2k_{1}},\hat{H}_{E}^{(k_{2})}(\mathfrak{r}(v_{2}))]]h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\hat{Z}\equiv

\displaystyle[h_{e_{z}^{+}},\hat{Q}_{v}^{2k_{5}}]h_{e_{z}^{+}}^{-1}.

Because of the holonomies therein, they are all operator-valued matrices whose entries, thus, will be denoted as
\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}
,
\hat{Y}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}
and
\hat{Z}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}
respectively. Then, one has

\hat{F}=\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}-\hat{Y}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{X}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}=\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}-\hat{Y}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{X}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}+[\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}]\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}.

(109)

According to Eq. (
33
), we first compute the expectation values of the operators with respect to coherent states labeled by
e^{iz_{e}\tau_{3}}
, and then gauge transform the results correspondingly. Hence, applying this procedure to the the first two terms of Eq. (
109
), one finally simplifies the subtraction of their expectation values as

\displaystyle\langle\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}-\hat{Y}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{X}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}\rangle_{\{g_{e}\}}=(\mathcal{C}_{abcdef}-\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{abcdef})\langle\hat{Y}_{ab}\hat{X}_{cd}\hat{Z}_{ef}\rangle_{\{z_{e}\}}

(110)

where
\mathcal{C}_{abcdef}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{abcdef}
are two sets of constant coefficients produced by the gauge-transformation.
Thanks to the the last equation, one has

\langle\hat{F}\rangle_{\{g_{e}\}}=(\mathcal{C}_{abcdef}-\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{abcdef})\langle\hat{Y}_{ab}\hat{X}_{cd}\hat{Z}_{ef}\rangle_{\{z_{e}\}}+\langle[\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}]\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}\rangle_{\{g_{e}\}}.

(111)

Regardless of the factors
\mathcal{C}_{abcdef}-\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{abcdef}
which is easy to compute, according to Eq. (
110
), the expectation values of
\langle\hat{Y}_{ab}\hat{X}_{cd}\hat{Z}_{ef}\rangle_{\{z_{e}\}}
and
\langle[\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}]\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}\rangle_{\{g_{e}\}}
are needed to compute to get
\langle\hat{F}\rangle_{\{g_{e}\}}
. While for the last term,
[\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}]\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
itself is of
O(t)
. Thus we only need to compute the leading-order term of
\langle[\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}]\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}\rangle
by applying Theorem
IV.2
. By definition of
\hat{F}
, without applying Eq. (
111
), we need to compute
\langle\hat{Y}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{X}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}\rangle
and
\langle\hat{Y}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\hat{X}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}\rangle
to get
\langle\hat{F}\rangle
. Comparing the computed terms before and after applying Eq. (
111
), it is concluded that the computational complexity is reduced due to Eq. (
111
), because it is much easier to compute
\langle[\hat{X}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},\hat{Y}_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}]\hat{Z}_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}\rangle
by applying Theorem
IV.2
.

To explicitly compute
\Delta\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}_{abcdef}-\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{abcdef}
, let us use
O_{i}
to denote expectation values of polynomials of fluxes and holonomies with respect to coherent states labeled by
e^{iz_{e}\tau_{3}}
.
Then
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
and the corresponding results of
\Delta\mathcal{C}
take the following forms, which are discussed case by case.

(1)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(1.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(1.2)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta_{1}}\tau^{\beta_{2}}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(1.3)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{\alpha_{2}}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
,

then
\Delta\mathcal{C}
is defined as the following,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{y})\left(\prod_{\beta=1}^{m_{y}}\tau^{\beta}\right)(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{x})\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{m_{x}}\tau^{\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\mathrm{tr}\left[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{x})\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{m_{x}}\tau^{\alpha}\right)(n_{x}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{y})\left(\prod_{\beta=1}^{m_{y}}\tau^{\beta}\right)\right]

where
(m_{y},m_{x})=(1,1)
,
(2,1)
and
(1,2)
for the cases (1.1), (1.2) and (1.3) respectively.

(2)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(2.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(2.2)

(n_{x}O_{1}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta_{1}}\tau^{\beta_{2}}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(2.3)

(n_{x}O_{1}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{\alpha_{2}}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
,

then
\Delta\mathcal{C}
is defined as the following,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=\left(\prod_{\beta=1}^{m_{y}}\tau^{\beta}\right)_{bc}\,\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{m_{x}}\tau^{\alpha}\right)_{da}\Big\{(n_{x}^{-1}n_{y})_{ab}[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{x})]_{cd}-(n_{y}^{-1}n_{x})_{cd}[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{y})_{ab}\Big\}

(3)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(3.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta_{1}}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta_{2}}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
,

then
\Delta\mathcal{C}
is defined as the following,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=(\tau^{\beta_{2}})_{bc}(\tau^{\alpha})_{da}\Big\{[n_{x}^{-1}n_{y}\tau^{\beta_{1}}]_{ab}[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{x})]_{cd}-[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1}n_{y}\tau^{\beta_{1}}]_{ab}[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{x})]_{cd}\Big\}

(4)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(4.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha_{2}}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
,

then
\Delta\mathcal{C}
is defined as the following,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=(\tau^{\alpha_{2}})_{da}(\tau^{\beta})_{bc}\Big\{[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{y})_{ab}[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1}n_{x}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}]_{cd}-[(n_{x}^{-1})n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{y})]_{ab}[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{x})\tau^{\alpha_{1}}]_{cd}\Big\}

(5)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(5.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(5.2)

(n_{x}O_{1}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta_{1}}\tau^{\beta_{2}}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(5.3)

(n_{x}O_{1}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{\alpha_{2}}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
,

then
\Delta\mathcal{C}
is defined as the following,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{m_{x}}\tau^{\alpha}\right)_{ba}\Big\{[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{y})\big(\prod_{\beta=1}^{m_{y}}\tau^{\beta}\big)(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{x})]_{ab}-[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{y})(\prod_{\beta=1}^{m_{y}}\tau^{\beta})(n_{y}^{-1}n_{x})]_{ab}\Big\}

(6)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(6.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(6.2)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta_{1}}\tau^{\beta_{2}}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
;

(6.3)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{\alpha_{2}}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
,

then
\Delta\mathcal{C}
is defined as the following,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=\left(\prod_{\beta=1}^{m_{y}}\tau^{\beta}\right)_{ba}\Big\{[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{x})(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{m_{x}}\tau^{\alpha})(n_{x}^{-1}n_{y})]_{ab}-[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{x})(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{m_{x}}\tau^{\alpha})(n_{x}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{y})]_{ab}\Big\}

(7)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(7.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta_{1}}h_{e_{y}^{+}}\tau^{\beta_{2}}h_{e_{y}^{+}}^{-1}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}
,

then
\Delta\mathcal{C}
is defined as the following,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=(\tau^{\beta_{2}})_{ba}\left\{[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{x})\tau^{\alpha}(n_{x}^{-1}n_{y})\tau^{\beta_{1}}]_{ab}-[(n_{y}^{-1}n_{x})\tau^{\alpha}(n_{x}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{y})\tau^{\beta_{1}}]_{ab}\right\}

(8)

If
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
is expressed in terms of the form

(8.1)

(n_{x}O_{1}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}h_{e_{x}^{+}}\tau^{\alpha_{2}}h_{e_{x}^{+}}^{-1}n_{x}^{-1})_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}(n_{y}O_{2}\tau^{\beta}n_{y}^{-1})_{\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}(n_{z}O_{3}\tau^{\gamma}n_{z}^{-1})_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}}

the subtraction of the coefficients are of the form

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{C}=(\tau^{\alpha_{2}})_{ba}\Big\{[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{y})\tau^{\beta}(n_{y}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{x})\tau^{\alpha_{1}}]_{ab}-[(n_{x}^{-1}n_{z})\tau^{\gamma}(n_{z}^{-1}n_{y})\tau^{\beta}n_{y}^{-1}n_{x}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}]_{ab}\Big\}.

In summary, let
N_{1}
be the original number of terms in
{}^{\rm alt}\hat{H}_{L}^{(k)}
. The symmetries implied by Eqs. (
77
) and (
79
) reduces this number to
N_{1}/4^{2}
, where the power 2 is from the fact that
{}^{\rm alt}\hat{H}_{L}^{(k)}
consists of two
\hat{H}_{E}
. Then Eq. (
81
) reduces this number further to
N_{1}/(4^{2}\times 48)
. Finally the symmetry (
104
) reduces this number to

\frac{N_{1}}{4^{2}\times 48\times 4}=\frac{N_{1}}{3072}.

Let
N_{2}
be the original number of terms in
{}^{\rm extr}\hat{H}_{L}^{(k)}
. The symmetries implied by Eqs. (
77
) and (
79
) are used at first to reduce this number to
N_{2}/4^{2}
. Then Eq. (
81
) reduces it to
N_{2}/(4^{2}\times 48)
. Finally, Eq. (
111
) reduce this number further to be about
5
5

5

The word “about” is because there exists cases with
\hat{X}=\hat{Y}
in Eq. (
109
).

\frac{N_{2}}{4^{2}\times 48\times 2}=\frac{N_{2}}{1536}.

V.2
Exhibit an explicit computation

In order to demonstrate the idea of our algorithm, some simple examples are used in this section. All of the cases illustrated by these examples finally occur in our computation. Particularly, some functions like
P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})
,
\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,k)})
and
\mathrm{WD}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,k)})
indeed exist in our codes
github
as the same manner.

Roughly speaking, the computation is divided into two steps. The first is to simplify the operator with applying the commutation relations (
13
) and the second is to compute the expectation values of the simplified operator.

In the first step, the non-commutative multiplications between operator make a non-trivial simplification. Because of the operator
\hat{Q}
, we actually need to deal with

\hat{P}^{\alpha}(v,i)=\hat{p}^{\alpha}_{s}(e^{+}_{v})-\hat{p}^{\alpha}_{t}(e^{-}_{v})

where
e^{\pm}_{v}
are the edges along the
i
th direction satisfying
s(e^{+}_{v})=v=t(e^{-}_{v})
. In the computation, we mainly need to deal with the commutators between
\hat{P}^{\alpha}(v,i)
and holonomy. Thus let us consider the example

\displaystyle[\prod_{j=1}^{m}\hat{P}^{\alpha_{j}}(v,i),h_{e_{v}^{+}}]=it\sum_{k=1}^{m}\tau^{\alpha_{k}}h_{e_{v}^{+}}\prod_{j\neq k}\hat{P}^{\alpha_{j}}(v,i)+(it)^{2}\sum_{k<l}\tau^{\alpha_{l}}\tau^{\alpha_{k}}h_{e_{v}^{+}}\prod_{j\neq k,l}\hat{P}^{\alpha_{j}}(v,i)+O(t^{3}).

(112)

Because these
\alpha_{j}
appear in the computed operator as dummy indices, their specific values do not matter in the operator-simplification procedure. In other words,
\prod_{j=1}^{m}\hat{P}^{\alpha_{j}}(v,i)
is treated like a tensor for which only the type does matter. Therefore, based on our algorithm, we define a function

P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}):=\prod_{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}}\hat{P}^{\alpha}(v,i),

(113)

where
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}
denote an index set of length
m
. In the supperscript of
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}
,
i
is used again for convenience so that the multiplication of
(\hat{P}^{\alpha}(v,1)\hat{P}^{\beta}(v,2)\hat{P}^{\gamma}(v,3))^{m}
occuring in
\hat{Q}_{v}^{m}
can be denoted as

(\hat{P}^{\alpha}(v,1)\hat{P}^{\beta}(v,2)\hat{P}^{\gamma}(v,3))^{m}=P(v,1,\mathcal{I}^{(1,m)})P(v,2,\mathcal{I}^{(2,m)})P(v,3,\mathcal{I}^{(3,m)}).

Similarly, let us define

\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,k)}):=\tau^{\alpha_{k}}\tau^{\alpha_{k-1}}\cdots\tau^{\alpha_{1}}

(114)

where
\mathcal{I}^{(i,k)}=\{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}\}
.
With these notions, Eq. (
112
) can be written as

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(it)\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathrm{WDt}(\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{(i,1)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}_{k}^{(i,m-1)})

(115)

\displaystyle+(it)^{2}\sum_{k<l}\mathrm{WDt}(\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k,l}^{(i,2)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}_{k,l}^{(i,m-2)})+O(t^{3})

where
\mathcal{I}_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{q}}^{(i,m)}
is the index set of
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}
without the
k_{1}
th,
k_{2}
th,
\cdots
,
k_{q}
th indices
and
\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{q}}^{(i,m)}
is the complement of
\mathcal{I}_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{q}}^{(i,m)}
.

Further, let us implement an non-operator factor
\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})
contracted with
P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})
to consider
\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})[P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}),h_{e_{v}^{+}}]
which is finally simplified as

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})[P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}),h_{e_{v}^{+}}]

(116)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(it)\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{F}(\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}\}_{(k)})

\displaystyle+(it)^{2}\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,2)}}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,2)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)})\sum_{k<l}\mathcal{F}(\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,2)}\}_{(k,l)})+O(t^{3}).

Here
\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-n)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,n)}\}_{(k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{n})}
is a joint set obtained by merging

\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-n)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,n)}
in such a way that the elements in
\mathcal{I}^{(i,n)}
(respecting their original order) are distributed in
k_{1}
th,
\cdots
,
k_{n}
th position in
\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-n)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,n)}\}_{(k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{n})}
.
Instead of deleting
n
indices in all possible
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}
, one replace this deleting procedure by an inserting procedure, namely merging all possible sets
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-n)}
with length
m-n
and
\mathcal{I}^{(i,n)}
with length
n
in the above way for all values of
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{n}
. Therefore, Eq. (
115
) can be simplified further as

\displaystyle[h_{e_{v}^{+}},P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})]\to

(117)

\displaystyle it\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})+(it)^{2}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,2)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)})+O(t^{3}).

Then, for each evaluation of the index sets
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-n)}
and
\mathcal{I}^{(i,n)}
, we joint them in the aforementioned way to consider
\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-n)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,n)}\}_{(k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{n})}
for all values of
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{n}
.

Similarly for the commutator
[h_{(e_{v}^{-})^{-1}},P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})]
, we have

\displaystyle[h_{(e_{v}^{-})^{-1}},P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})]\to

(118)

\displaystyle-it\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})+(-it)^{2}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,2)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)})+O(t^{3}).

For
[h_{e_{v}^{-}},P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})]
and
[h_{(e_{v}^{+})^{-1}},P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})]
, we need to define

\mathrm{WD}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,k)}):=\tau^{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{\alpha_{1}}\cdots\tau^{\alpha_{k}}

(119)

where
\mathcal{I}^{(i,k)}=\{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}\}
.

\displaystyle[h_{e_{v}^{-}},P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})]\to

(120)

\displaystyle it\mathrm{WD}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})+(it)^{2}\mathrm{WD}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,2)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)})+O(t^{3}),

\displaystyle[h_{(e_{v}^{+})^{-1}},P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})]\to

\displaystyle-it\mathrm{WD}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})+(-it)^{2}\mathrm{WD}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,2)})h_{e_{v}^{+}}P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)})+O(t^{3}).

The second step is to compute the expectation value of the simplified operator. Let us still take
\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})[P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}),h_{e_{v}^{+}}]
as an example.
A subtlety here is that the operator
\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)})[P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m)}),h_{e_{v}^{+}}]
involves two edges
e^{\pm}_{v}
because of the operator
\hat{P}^{\alpha}(v,i)
. We consider its expectation value with respect to coherent state
|\psi_{g_{e_{v}^{+}}}\rangle\otimes|\psi_{g_{e_{v}^{-}}}\rangle
, where, without loss of generality, we set

g_{e_{v}^{+}}=g_{e_{v}^{-}}=g=ne^{iz\tau_{3}}n^{-1}.

According to the above discussion,
one can apply the result of Eq. (
116
) to compute the expectation value of its LHS.
Let us take the first term for instance. We need to compute the expectation value of

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)},b}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)},a,b)\left\langle D^{1/2}_{bc}(h_{e_{v}^{+}})P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})\right\rangle_{g}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{F}(\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}\}_{(k)})

(121)

Applying Eq. (
33
), we finally obtain the following

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)},b}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)},a,b)\left\langle D^{1/2}_{bc}(h_{e_{v}^{+}})P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})\right\rangle_{g}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{F}(\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}\}_{(k)})

(122)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)},b}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)},a,b)D^{1/2}_{bb^{\prime}}(n)\left\langle D^{1/2}_{b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(h_{e_{v}^{+}})P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})\right\rangle_{z}

\displaystyle D^{1/2}_{c^{\prime}c}(n^{-1})\sum_{k=1}^{m}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}\}_{(k)})

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is given by

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(\{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}\}):=\sum_{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{k}}\mathcal{F}(\{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{k}\})D^{1}_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}(n^{-1})\cdots D^{1}_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}(n^{-1}).

To compute Eq. (
122
), the results of Theorem
IV.2
can be applied. Thus, one obtains the possible
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)}
are:

(i)

If one only computes the leading-order term of the expectation value, then
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)}
is evaluated at
\underbrace{\{0,0,\cdots,0\}}_{m-1}

(ii)

If one only computes the expectation value up to
O(t)
, then
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)}
is evaluated at
\underbrace{\{0,0,\cdots,0\}}_{m-1}
,
\underbrace{\{0,0,\cdots,0,\pm 1\}}_{m-1}
,
\underbrace{\{0,0,\cdots,0,1,-1\}}_{m-1}
and
\underbrace{\{0,0,\cdots,0,-1,1\}}_{m-1}
.

With this discussion, Eq. (
122
) can be computed by considering both the expectation-value part

\left\langle D^{1/2}_{b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(h_{e_{v}^{+}})P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})\right\rangle_{z}

and the non-operator-factor part

\sum_{b}\mathrm{WDt}(\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)},a,b)D^{1/2}_{bb^{\prime}}(n)D^{1/2}_{c^{\prime}c}(n^{-1})\sum_{k=1}^{m}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}\}_{(k)})

for each possibility of
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)}
.

For the expectation-value part, the values of
\left\langle D^{1/2}_{b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(h_{e_{v}^{+}})P(v,i,\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)})\right\rangle_{z}
for Case (i) and the cases where
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)}
contains
\pm 1
in Case (ii) are computed by Eq. (
65
). Furthermore, for the cases where
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)}
contains
\pm 1
in Case (ii), the results are independent of the position of
\pm 1
in
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-1)}
. Therefore, only
1+2+2=5
cases are considered in Case (ii) finally. Comparing with the original number of cases
3^{3m-1}
, one can obtain an advantage of our algorithm.

For the non-operator-factor part, given a possible
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)}
and consider all possible
\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}
s. Then all possible
\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}\}_{(k)}
s can be reconstructed. It is noted that, for Case (ii), the positions of
\pm 1
in
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)}
does matter to the value of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
. Thus we need to consider the permutations of indices in (with keeping the relative order between
\pm 1
)
\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)}
in Case (ii) when reconstructing
\{\mathcal{I}^{(i,m-2)},\mathcal{I}^{(i,1)}\}_{(k)}
. With the results of these two parts, the results of Eq.(
121
) is obtained correspondingly.

Finally, we complete this section with the discussion on the values of
n
and
\vec{k}
in
\hat{H}_{E}^{(n)}
and
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
, which is summarized as the following lemma

Lemma V.1
.

To get the expectation values of
\hat{H}_{E}^{(n)}
and
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
up to order
O(t)
, it is sufficient to set
n
and
\vec{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4},k_{5})
respectively such that

n\leq 3

(123)

and

\frac{|k_{1}+k_{2}-3|+(k_{1}+k_{2}-3)}{2}+\frac{|k_{3}+k_{4}-3|+(k_{3}+k_{4}-3)}{2}+k_{5}\leq 3.

(124)

The proof of this lemma is sketched as follows.
When one replaces
\hat{V}_{v}
in
\widehat{H_{E}}
by
\hat{V}_{GT}^{(v)}
, the term
\left(\hat{Q}^{2}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle^{2}-1\right)^{k}
in
\hat{V}_{GT}^{(v)}
contributes as the following

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{tr}(h_{\alpha_{IJ}}h_{s_{K}}[\left(\hat{Q}^{2}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle^{2}-1\right)^{k},h_{s_{K}}^{-1}])

(125)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}\mathrm{tr}(h_{\alpha_{IJ}}h_{s_{K}}\left(\hat{Q}^{2}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle^{2}-1\right)^{m}\frac{[\hat{Q}^{2}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle^{2},h_{s_{K}}^{-1}]}{t}\left(\hat{Q}^{2}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle^{2}-1\right)^{k-1-m})

where the overall coefficient is neglected. Since
\hat{Q}^{2}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle^{2}-1=\left(\hat{Q}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle+1\right)\left(\hat{Q}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle-1\right)
,
and
\langle\hat{Q}/\langle\hat{Q}\rangle-1\rangle=O(t)
,
Theorem
IV.1
can be applied, which indicates that the leading-order expectation value of the operator (
125
) is at least
O(t^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor})
.
Thus, as far as expanding the expectation value to
O(t)
is concerning, one can sufficiently choose
k\leq 3
,
which leads to
n\leq 3
in
\hat{H}_{E}^{(n)}
. A very similar discussion for
\hat{H}_{L}^{(\vec{k})}
can be done, which gives us Eq. (
124
).

VI
Quantum correction in the expectation value

The resulting expectation value of the Hamiltonian with unit lapse
\widehat{H[1]}=\widehat{H_{E}}+\left(1+\beta^{2}\right)\widehat{H_{L}}
at coherent states with cosmological data (
\eta<0
in our convention) is shown as follows
6
6

6

Here, without loss of generality, the graph
\gamma
is assumed to be a cubic lattice in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
and has only a single vertex.

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{H_{E}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6a\sqrt{-\beta\eta}\sin^{2}(\xi)-\frac{3}{4}at\sqrt{-\frac{\beta}{\eta^{3}}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\xi}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\xi}{2}\right)\Bigg\{\cos\left(\frac{\xi}{2}\right)\Big[8\eta^{2}+8\eta(4\cosh(\eta)-3)\text{csch}(\eta)-9\Big]

(126)

\displaystyle-\,12i\eta\sin\left(\frac{\xi}{2}\right)\Bigg\}+O(t^{2}),

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{6a\sqrt{-\beta\eta}\sin^{2}(\xi)\cos^{2}(\xi)}{\beta^{2}}-\frac{3at}{262144(-\beta\eta)^{3/2}}\Bigg\{2\left(3-220\eta^{2}\right)\cos(6\xi)

(127)

\displaystyle+\,4i\eta(4838\sin(\xi)-6284\sin(2\xi)+4685\sin(3\xi)-5222\sin(4\xi)-105\sin(5\xi))

\displaystyle+\,2(-3611+8\eta(492\eta+11i))\cos(\xi)-2(-789+4\eta(305\eta+18i))\cos(2\xi)

\displaystyle+\,(4413-4\eta(928\eta+49i))\cos(3\xi)+8(-1978+\eta(4192\eta-7i))\cos(4\xi)

\displaystyle+\,(-7+4(-272\eta+5i)\eta)\cos(5\xi)-4\eta\coth(\eta)\Big[536\cos(\xi)+1731\cos(2\xi)

\displaystyle+\,1524\cos(3\xi)-40548\cos(4\xi)+116\cos(5\xi)+117\cos(6\xi)+37292\Big]

\displaystyle+\,8\eta\text{csch}(\eta)\Big[130\cos(\xi)+918\cos(2\xi)+801\cos(3\xi)-18618\cos(4\xi)

\displaystyle+\,125\cos(5\xi)+58\cos(6\xi)+16362\Big]+8(1436+\eta(-4056\eta+25i))\Bigg\}+O(t^{2}).

According to (
97
),
\langle\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle=\langle{}^{\rm extr}\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle+\langle{}^{\rm alt}\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle
, in which
\langle{}^{\rm alt}\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle
is,

\displaystyle\langle{}^{\rm alt}\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3a\sqrt{-\beta\eta}\sin^{2}(2\xi)}{8\beta^{2}}-\frac{3at}{32768(-\beta\eta)^{3/2}}\Bigg\{4\left(104\eta^{2}-79\right)\cos(\xi)+\left(68-160\eta^{2}\right)\cos(2\xi)

(128)

\displaystyle+4\left(55-104\eta^{2}\right)\cos(3\xi)+\left(944\eta^{2}-501\right)\cos(4\xi)-848\eta^{2}

\displaystyle-2\eta\Big[\coth(\eta)(-(262\cos(\xi)-6(46\cos(2\xi)+65\cos(3\xi)-380\cos(4\xi)+366)))

\displaystyle-\text{csch}(\eta)(-292\cos(\xi)+268\cos(2\xi)+420\cos(3\xi)-2035\cos(4\xi)+1799)

\displaystyle-64i(6\sin(\xi)-10\sin(2\xi)+6\sin(3\xi)-7\sin(4\xi))\Big]+241\Bigg\}+O(t^{2}),

and
\langle{}^{\rm extr}\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle
is

\displaystyle\langle{}^{\rm extr}\widehat{H_{L}}\rangle=

\displaystyle-\frac{9a\sqrt{-\beta\eta}\sin^{2}(2\xi)}{8\beta^{2}}+\frac{3at}{262144(-\beta\eta)^{3/2}}\Bigg\{-2\left(580\eta^{2}+72i\eta-517\right)\cos(2\xi)

(129)

\displaystyle+2\left(3-220\eta^{2}\right)\cos(6\xi)+4i\eta\Big[3302\sin(\xi)-3724\sin(2\xi)+3149\sin(3\xi)

\displaystyle-35(98\sin(4\xi)+3\sin(5\xi))\Big]+2(-2347+8\eta(284\eta+11i))\cos(\xi)

\displaystyle+(2653-4\eta(96\eta+49i))\cos(3\xi)+56(-211+\eta(464\eta-i))\cos(4\xi)

\displaystyle+(-7+4(-272\eta+5i)\eta)\cos(5\xi)-4\eta\coth(\eta)\Big[1584\cos(\xi)+627\cos(2\xi)

\displaystyle-36\cos(3\xi)-31428\cos(4\xi)+116\cos(5\xi)+117\cos(6\xi)+28508\Big]

\displaystyle+8\eta\text{csch}(\eta)\Big[714\cos(\xi)+382\cos(2\xi)-39\cos(3\xi)

\displaystyle-14548\cos(4\xi)+125\cos(5\xi)+58\cos(6\xi)+12764\Big]+8\eta(-3208\eta+25i)+9560\Bigg\}+O(t^{2}).

When expressing
\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle
to be
\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle=H_{0}+tH_{1}+O(t^{2})
, we notice that the
O(t)
-term
H_{1}
contains
\eta
in its denominator and, thus, is divergent if
\eta\to 0
.
This feature is implied by the fact that if
\langle\hat{Q}_{v}\rangle\to 0
,
\hat{V}_{GT}^{(v)}
is divergent. This is because when if
\eta\to 0
,
\langle\hat{Q}_{v}\rangle\to 0
since
\langle\hat{Q}_{v}\rangle\sim|\eta|^{3}
.

Hence, the expansion of
\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle
becomes invalid when
\eta
is too small. More precisely, expressing
\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle
as
\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle=\sqrt{|\eta|}\left[\mathfrak{f}_{0}+({t}/{\eta^{2}})\,\mathfrak{f}_{1}+O(t^{2})\right]
, we get that
\mathfrak{f}_{0}
is independent of
\eta
, and
\mathfrak{f}_{1}
is regular at
\eta\to 0
. Thus, it is concluded that our expansion is valid when
\eta^{2}\gg t
. This aspect is important for a new improvement of cosmological effective dynamics derived from the full LQG
Han:2021cwb
. The expansion is valid for large
|\eta|
, because
\mathfrak{f}_{1}
is regular at
|\eta|\to\infty
.

Consider the reduced-phase-space LQG of gravity coupled to Gaussian dust. Then, the relational evolution with respect to the dust time
T
will be generated by the physical Hamiltonian
\widehat{\bf H}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\widehat{H[1]}+\widehat{H[1}]^{\dagger}\right)
. Its coherent state expectation value reads

\langle\widehat{\bf H}\rangle=\Re(\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle)\equiv H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}+O(t^{2}),\quad\widetilde{H}_{1}=\Re(H_{1}).

(130)

In order to demonstrate the physical application and effects from the
O(\hbar)
correction we adopt the proposal in
Dapor:2017rwv
as follows. Firstly, we view
\langle\widehat{\bf H}\rangle
as the effective Hamiltonian on the 2-dimensional phase space
\mathscr{P}_{cos}
of homogeneous and isotropic cosmology. Then, one can verify that
\eta=-\frac{\mu^{2}P_{0}}{\beta a^{2}}
and
\xi=\mu\beta K_{0}
where
\mu
is the coordinate length of
e\in E(\gamma)
,
P_{0}
is the square of the scale factor and
K_{0}
is the extrinsic curvature. Thus, the Poisson bracket between
\xi
and
\eta
reads

\{\eta,\xi\}=\frac{\kappa}{3a^{2}}
. With this Poisson bracket, the Hamiltonian time evolution on
\mathscr{P}_{cos}
generated by
\langle\widehat{\bf H}\rangle
is computable. The numerical result is shown in Fig.
1
, which respectively depicts the dynamics of the spatial volume governed by
H_{0}
,
\langle\widehat{\bf H}\rangle=H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}
and the classical FLRW Hamiltonian
H_{cl}=-6a\beta^{-3/2}\sqrt{-\eta}\,\xi^{2}
.

Figure 1:
Evolution of the spatial volume generated by
\langle\widehat{\bf H}\rangle=H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}
in (
130
) (black curve),
H_{0}
(red dashed curve), and
H_{cl}
(black circles).
The coincided initial data of
\eta,d\eta/dT
are chosen to be at
T=0
for these 3 cases. The parameters are set to be
a=1
,
t=10^{-5}
, and
\beta=0.2375
.

Figure 2:

Plots of the critical density
\rho_{c}
of the dynamics governed by
H_{0}
(red circle) and,
H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}
with
t=10^{-5}
(dash-dotted curve)
t=0.01
(solid curve). The parameters are set as
a=1
and
\beta=0.2375
.

In the example shown in Fig.
1
, a relatively small
t=10^{-5}
is chosen to display the effects of the next-to-leading-order term. The coincided initial data of
\eta,d\eta/dT
are chosen to be at
T=0
for all of the three cases. Since
\eta|_{T=0}
gives a large spatial volume,
T=0
is in the low-energy-density regime.
As shown in Fig.
1
, toward
T<0
, the evolution with respect to
H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}
is similar as that corresponding to
H_{0}
, where the latter one gives the
\mu_{0}
-scheme effective dynamics.
Both of the effective Hamiltonian
H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}
and
H_{0}
resolve the big-bang singularity by “a bounce”. Moreover, the dynamics of
H_{0}
and
H_{cl}
at late time
T>0
are compatible. Further more, it is shown in Fig.
1
that
t\widetilde{H}_{1}
in
H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}
behaves like an additional matter distribution with negative
energy density of
O(t)
, which causes the universe to re-collapse and have another bounce at very late time. It should be noted that the time of re-collapse is extremely late because of the tiny value of
t
. It can be verified numerically that the time when the recollapse happens is correlated to
t
, namely, the recollapse happens earlier for a larger
t
but happens later for a smaller
t
. For vanishing
t
, it needs infinitely long time before the recollapse happens, i.e. there is no recollapse. Finally, the collapse occurs in the FLRW phase with
\xi\ll 1
. Thus expanding
\widetilde{H}_{1}
at
\xi=0
, we have

\widetilde{H}_{1}\cong\frac{3a(-\beta\eta)^{3/2}\left(1280\eta^{2}-3072\eta\coth(\eta)-1792\eta\text{csch}(\eta)-5568\right)}{262144\beta^{3}\eta^{3}}+O\left(\xi^{2}\right).

(131)

That is, when
\xi
is vanishing,
\widetilde{H}_{1}
contains terms which do not vanish. It is these terms that lead to the recollapse. These terms are all contributed by the Lorentzian part, and is the consequence from the Thiemann’s regularization of the Lorentzian Hamiltonian.

For the critical density
\rho_{c}
plotted in Fig.
2
, it also receives correction from
t\widetilde{H}_{1}
at the
T<0
bounce.
As is known, the critical density from the
\mu_{0}
-scheme dynamics governed by
H_{0}
reads
\rho_{c}=\frac{3}{2a^{2}\kappa\beta^{3}\left(\beta^{2}+1\right)|\eta_{b}|}
with
|\eta_{b}|
being the value of
|\eta|
at the bounce. When
\widetilde{H}_{1}
term is considered, the dependence of
\rho_{c}
on
\eta_{b}
is only known numerically. Some numerical results are presented in Fig.
2
.
As shown there, instead of blowing up for small
|\eta_{b}|
, the corrected
\rho_{c}
from
\langle\widehat{\bf H}\rangle=H_{0}+t\widetilde{H}_{1}
is bounded from above for small values of
|\eta_{b}|
.
In an optimistic viewpoint, this correction of
\rho_{c}
might hint that the correction from higher-order term in
t
could potentially flatten the dependence
of
\eta_{b}
in
\rho_{c}
. This flattened behavior of
\rho_{c}
is also supported by the current model of
\bar{\mu}
-scheme effective dynamics with complete quantum corrections, which is considered as an important feature of
\bar{\mu}
-scheme Loop Quantum Cosmology. However, by recalling the fact that our expansion in
t
requires
\eta^{2}\gg t
, one realizes that the small
|\eta_{b}|
regime, where the correction of
\rho_{c}
becomes significant, mostly violates this requirement (see fig.
2
). Hence the quantum dynamics near the bounce is still an open problem from this point of view.

It should be emphasized that the proposal
Dapor:2017rwv
adopted here for studying the effective dynamics is not as rigorous as the path integral formula (
2
). As argued in Section
I
, the
O(t)
correction in
\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle
is only a partial contribution in the quantum effective action that ultimately determining the quantum effect in the dynamics. Hence, the cosmological dynamics plotted in Fig.
1
only shows
O(t)
correction in
\langle\widehat{H[1]}\rangle
from one of the three
O(t)
contributions in
\Gamma
, and is not yet a rigorous prediction from the principle of LQG. We have only focused on non-gauge-invariant coherent state and neglected the group averaging that impose the gauge invariance in the path integral (
2
). Moreover, this paper only focus on the effects of the quantum correction of linear order in
t
, and the effects from higher order corrections are still unclear and beyond the scope of this paper.

VII
Conclusion and outlook

In this paper, we developed an algorithm to overcome the complexity of computing the expectation value of LQG Hamiltonian operator
\widehat{H[N]}
. With this algorithm, the
O(\hbar)
correction in the expectation value
\langle\widehat{H[N]}\rangle
at the coherent state peaked at the homogeneous and isotropic data of cosmology is computed. In the current work, there are several perspectives which should be addressed in the future:

The first one is to complete the computation of the quantum effective action
\Gamma
mentioned in Section
I
.
After completing of this current work, the only missing ingredient in the
O(\hbar)
terms of
\Gamma
is the “1-loop determinant”
\det(\mathfrak{H})
. Therefore, a research to be carried out immediately is to compute this correction of
\det(\mathfrak{H})
at the homogeneous and isotropic background.
Once we obtain all of the
O(\hbar)
contribution to
\Gamma
, the variation of
\Gamma
should give the quantum corrected effective equations which will demonstrate the quantum correction to the cosmological model implied by LQG.

The next step of generalizing our computation is to study the expectation values of
\langle\widehat{H[N]}\rangle
with respect to the coherent states peaked at cosmological perturbations. The semiclassical limits of the expectation value and the cosmological perturbation theory from the path integral (
2
) have been studied in
Han:2020iwk
.
Thus, it is interesting to study the
O(\ell_{\rm p})
correction to the cosmological perturbation theory.

Finally, the computation of the quantum correction in
\langle\widehat{H[N]}\rangle
should also be extended to the model of gravity coupled to standard matter fields. The contributions of matter fields to
\widehat{H[N]}
have been studied in
Thiemann:1997rt
;
Sahlmann:2002qj
. Since the matter parts in
\widehat{H[N]}
is much simpler than the Lorentzian part in
\widehat{H[N]}
, the computation of their expectation values should not be hard. Study of the matter contributions and their quantum corrections is a project currently undergoing
inpreparation
.

Our work expands
\langle\widehat{H[N]}\rangle
to the order
t^{1}
and neglects the higher order terms. It is important to understand higher order contributions, or namely the reminder of the expansion, in order to rigorously estimate the regime in the phase space where the expansion is good (see e.g. the paragraph above (
130
) for the discussion of the phase space regime such that
t\widetilde{H}_{1}
is small).

Appendix A
SL(
2,\mathbb{C}
) and SU(2) groups

Let
\sigma_{i}
with
i=1,2,3
be the Pauli matrices and
\tau_{k}:=-i\sigma_{k}/2
. Define

\vec{\theta}=(\theta\sin(\psi)\cos(\phi),\theta\sin(\psi)\sin(\phi),\theta\cos(\psi)).

(132)

h\in
SU(2) can be coordinatized as

h=e^{\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\tau}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cos\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)-i\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\cos(\psi)&-i\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin(\psi)e^{-i\phi}\\
-i\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin(\psi)e^{i\phi}&\cos\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)+i\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\cos(\psi)\\
\end{array}\right)

(133)

with
\theta,\ \phi\in(0,2\pi)
and
\psi\in(0,\pi)
. The Haar measure then is

{\rm d}\mu_{H}(\vec{\theta})=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta}{2})\sin(\psi){\rm d}\theta{\rm d}\psi{\rm d}\phi.

(134)

Moreover, by defining

\vec{p}=(p\sin(\alpha)\cos(\beta),p\sin(\alpha)\sin(\beta),p\cos(\alpha))

(135)

with
p>0
,
\alpha\in(0,\pi)
and
\beta\in(0,2\pi)
,

g\in\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
can be parameterized as

g(\vec{p},\vec{\theta})=e^{i\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\tau}}=:e^{i\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\tau}}h(\vec{\theta}).

(136)

For each
p>0
, there exists
u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}\in\mathrm{SU}(2)
such that

(u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm})^{-1}\,\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\tau}\,(u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm})=\pm p\tau_{3}.

(137)

Note that
u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}
is determined by Eq. (
137
) up to a right transformation by
e^{\alpha\tau_{3}}
. Namely
u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}e^{\alpha\tau_{3}}
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
are solution to Eq. (
137
) provided
u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}
does. Moreover,
u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}
has the relation

u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}=u_{-\vec{p}}^{\mp}.

(138)

Let us denote
\eta\equiv\pm p
and
u\equiv u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}
for convenience. Then

g(\vec{p},\vec{\theta})=u\,e^{i\eta\tau_{3}}\,u^{-1}h(\vec{\theta}).

(139)

For
u^{-1}h(\vec{\theta})\in
SU(2), decomposing it as

u^{-1}h(\vec{\theta})=e^{-\xi\tau_{3}}n^{-1},

(140)

one get

g(\vec{p},\vec{\theta})=ue^{i(\eta+i\xi)\tau_{3}}n^{-1}.

(141)

Note that Eq. (
140
) determines
n
up to a right transformation by
e^{\alpha\tau_{3}}
as that for
u_{\vec{p}}^{\pm}
.
n
satisfies

n(\eta\tau_{3})n^{-1}=h(\vec{\theta})(\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\tau})h(\vec{\theta})^{-1}.

(142)

The Wigner 3-
j
symbol
\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{3}\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m_{3}\end{array}\right)
is an SU(2)-invariant tensor, namely

D^{j_{1}}_{n_{1}m_{1}}(h)D^{j_{2}}_{n_{2}m_{2}}(h)D^{j_{3}}_{n_{3}m_{3}}(h)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{3}\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m_{3}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{3}\\
n_{1}&n_{2}&n_{3}\end{array}\right),\ \forall h\in\mathrm{SU}(2).

(143)

Define
\tau_{\alpha}
with
\alpha=-1,0,1
as

\tau_{\pm 1}=\mp\frac{\tau_{1}\pm i\tau_{2}}{\sqrt{2}},\ \tau_{0}=\tau_{3}.

(144)

We obtain the
j
-representation of
\tau_{\alpha}
in terms of the 3
j
symbol, according to the Wigner–Eckart theorem, as

D^{{}^{\prime}j}_{mm^{\prime}}(\tau_{\alpha})=iw_{j}\epsilon^{j}_{nm}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j&j&1\\
n&m^{\prime}&\alpha\end{array}\right)

(145)

where
w_{j}=\sqrt{j(j+1)(2j+1)}
and
\epsilon^{j}_{nm}=(-1)^{j+m}\delta(n,-m)
is the 2-
j
symbol. The 2-
j
symbol is also SU(2) invariant. By 3-
j
symbol and
\epsilon^{j}_{nm}
, any SU(2)-intertwiner can be constructed as

\iota(k_{1}k_{2}\cdots k_{n-1})_{m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}\cdots m_{n}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&k_{1}\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&l_{1}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{k_{1}}_{l_{1}l_{1}^{\prime}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}k_{1}&j_{3}&k_{2}\\
l_{1}^{\prime}&m_{3}&l_{2}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{k_{2}}_{l_{2}l_{2}^{\prime}}\cdots\epsilon^{k_{n-2}}_{l_{n-2}l_{n-2}^{\prime}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}k_{n-2}&j_{n-1}&j_{n}\\
l_{n-2}^{\prime}&m_{n-1}&m_{n}\end{array}\right).

(146)

Moreover, the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients relates to
3j
-symbol as

\displaystyle\langle j_{1}m_{1}j_{2}m_{2}|JM\rangle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{2j_{2}}\sqrt{2J+1}\,\epsilon^{J}_{MN}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}J&j_{2}&j_{1}\\
N&m_{2}&m_{1}\end{array}\right)

(147)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{j_{1}-j_{2}-J}\sqrt{2J+1}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&J\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&N\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{J}_{NM}

Appendix B
the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients with negative parameters

Given
j_{1}
,
m_{1}
,
j_{2}
,
m_{2}
and
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
, the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients
\langle j_{1}m_{1}j_{2}m_{2}|pm\rangle\equiv\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&p\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]
for various
p
satisfy the difference equation
natura1988nature

A(p+1)\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&p+1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]+A(p)\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&p-1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]+(A_{0}(p)-m_{1}+m_{2})\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&p\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=0

(148)

where
\max(|m|,|j_{1}-j_{2}|)\leq p\leq j_{1}+j_{2}
and

\displaystyle A(p)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\sqrt{-\frac{(p^{2}-\xi_{1}^{2})(p^{2}-\xi_{2}^{2})(p^{2}-\xi_{3}^{2})}{4p^{2}-1}},

(149)

\displaystyle A_{0}(p)=

\displaystyle\frac{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}{p(p+1)}

with

\xi_{1}=j_{1}-j_{2},\ \xi_{2}=j_{1}+j_{2}+1,\ \xi_{3}=m.

With the initial data

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{1}+j_{2}\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=\sqrt{\frac{\left(2j_{1}\right)!\left(2j_{2}\right)!\left(j_{1}+j_{2}-m\right)!\left(j_{1}+j_{2}+m\right)!}{\left(2j_{1}+2j_{2}\right)!\left(j_{1}-m_{1}\right)!\left(j_{1}+m_{1}\right)!\left(j_{2}-m_{2}\right)!\left(j_{2}+m_{2}\right)!}}

(150)

the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients for other values of
p
are computable with Eq. (
148
).

In order to extend the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients to negative parameters, we define a function

\mathcal{C}_{0}(x):=\sqrt{\frac{\left(2j_{2}\right)!\,\Gamma(2x+1)\Gamma(x+j_{2}-m+1)\Gamma(x+j_{2}+m+1)}{\left(j_{2}-m_{2}\right)!\left(j_{2}+m_{2}\right)!\Gamma(2x+2j_{2}+1)\Gamma(x-m_{1}+1)\Gamma(x+m_{1}+1)}},

(151)

with which

\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{1}+j_{2}\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=\mathcal{C}_{0}(j_{1}).

(152)

It is remarkable that
\mathcal{C}_{0}(x)
is well-defined not only for positive
x
such that

x-m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z},\ x\geq|m_{1}|

(153)

but also for negative
x
satisfying

x-m_{1}\in\mathbb{N},\ x\leq\min(-|m_{1}|,-j_{2}-|m|-1)

(154)

where those gamma functions with negative integers is understood as

\frac{\Gamma(-m_{1})\cdots\Gamma(-m_{k})}{\Gamma(-n_{1})\cdots\Gamma(-n_{k})}=\lim_{z\to 0}\frac{\Gamma(z-m_{1})\cdots\Gamma(z-m_{k})}{\Gamma(z-n_{1})\cdots\Gamma(z-n_{k})}=(-1)^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}-m_{1}-\cdots-m_{k}}\frac{n_{1}!\cdots n_{k}!}{m_{1}!\cdots m_{k}!}.

(155)

By definition, the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients
\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{1}+j_{2}-\iota\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]
is obtained by applying the recurrence relation (
148
)
successively for
\iota
steps with the initial data
\mathcal{C}_{0}(j_{1},j_{1}+j_{2})
.
Then, we defined

\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-j_{1}&j_{2}&-j_{1}+j_{2}-\iota\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]
, the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients with negative parameters, as the result by applying the recurrence relation

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-j_{1}&j_{2}&-q-1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=-\frac{1}{\tilde{A}(-q)}\left(\tilde{A}(-q+1)\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-j_{1}&j_{2}&-q+1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]+(\tilde{A}_{0}(-q)-m_{1}+m_{2})\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-j_{1}&j_{2}&-q\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]\right)

(156)

with the initial data
\mathcal{C}_{0}(-j_{1})
, where

\tilde{A}(q)=A(q)\big|_{j_{1}\to-j_{1}},\ \tilde{A}_{0}(q)=A_{0}(q)\big|_{j_{1}\to-j_{1}}.

(157)

This definition extended the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients to negative parameters. It guarantees that,

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-j_{1}&j_{2}&-j_{1}+j_{2}-\iota\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=\left.\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{1}+j_{2}-\iota\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]\right|_{j_{1}\to-j_{1}}.

(158)

By definition, it has

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{0}(-j_{1})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\left(2j_{1}-2j_{2}-1\right)!\left(2j_{2}\right)!\left(j_{1}-m_{1}-1\right)!\left(j_{1}+m_{1}-1\right)!}{\left(2j_{1}-1\right)!\left(j_{1}-j_{2}-m-1\right)!\left(j_{1}-j_{2}+m-1\right)!\left(j_{2}-m_{2}\right)!\left(j_{2}+m_{2}\right)!}}

(159)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{j_{2}+{m_{2}}}\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}-1&j_{2}&j_{1}-j_{2}-1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right].

Moreover,
\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}-1&j_{2}&j_{1}-j_{2}+\iota-1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]
can also be obtained by applying successively the recurrence relation

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&q\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=-\frac{1}{B(q)}\left(B(q-1)\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&q-2\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]+(B_{0}(q-1)-m_{1}+m_{2})\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&q-1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]\right)

(160)

with the initial data
\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{1}-j_{2}-1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]
, where

B(q)=A(q)\big|_{j_{1}\to j_{1}-1},\ B_{0}(q):=A_{0}(q)\Big|_{j_{1}\to j_{1}-1}.

(161)

Furthermore, it can be verified that

B(q)=-\tilde{A}(-q),\ B_{0}(q-1)=\tilde{A}_{0}(-q).

(162)

Therefore, according to Eqs. (
159
), (
156
) and (
160
), we finally have

\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-j_{1}&j_{2}&-j_{1}+j_{2}-\iota\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=(-1)^{j_{2}+m_{2}-\iota}\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}-1&j_{2}&j_{1}-j_{2}+\iota-1\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right],

(163)

namely

\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-j_{1}&j_{2}&-j_{1}+\Delta\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right]=(-1)^{\Delta+m_{2}}\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}j_{1}-1&j_{2}&j_{1}-1-\Delta\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m\end{array}\right].

(164)

)

One can refer to

The 2-

The

-symbols is graphically represented as

The Wigner-D matrix

For the multiplication operator

\displaystyle D^{\iota}_{ab}(h_{e})D^{j}_{mn}(h_{e})=\sum_{J={j-\iota}}^{j+\iota}d_{J}(-1)^{M-N}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\iota&j&J\\

(168)

3j

(170)

where we used (

). Thus , one has

p^{\alpha}_{s}(e)\mathord{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{dhe}}}}=-tw_{j}\mathord{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{psdhe}}}}

(171)

\hat{F}_{e}

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}_{e}\rangle_{z_{e}}=\sum_{jj^{\prime}}d_{j}e^{-\frac{t}{2}(j(j+1)+j^{\prime}(j^{\prime}+1))}\mathord{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{expectF4}}}}.

(174)

), we claim that

\displaystyle\mathord{\vbox{\hbox{\raisebox{0.1pt}{\includegraphics[width]{pspth1}}}}}=e^{(k-a+\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{m})\overline{z_{e}}}\mathord{\vbox{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\includegraphics[width]{pspth0}}}}},

.

), where the factor

Appendix D
Expectation value of
\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}

D.1
for the case with
\iota=0

Let us consider

\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{000}\equiv\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}=\hat{p}_{s}^{\alpha_{1}}(e)\cdots\hat{p}_{s}^{\alpha_{m}}(e).

(178)

Eq. (
170
) gives us that

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}=t^{m}\sum_{j\geq 1/2}d_{j}e^{-tj(j+1)}F_{0}(j,\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh((2j+1)\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

(179)

where the function
F_{0}
is given by

\displaystyle F_{0}(j,k)=

\displaystyle(-w_{j})^{m}\epsilon^{j}_{kk_{1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j&1&j\\
k_{1}&\alpha_{1}&n_{1}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j}_{n_{1}k_{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j&1&j\\
k_{2}&\alpha_{2}&n_{2}\end{array}\right)\cdots

(180)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n_{m-2}k_{m-1}}^{j}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j&1&j\\
k_{m-1}&\alpha_{m-1}&n_{m-1}\end{array}\right)\epsilon_{n_{m-1}k_{m}}^{j}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j&1&j\\
k_{m}&\alpha_{m}&k\end{array}\right)

By Eqs. (
147
) and (
164
), we achieve

\displaystyle\left.w_{j}\epsilon^{j}_{\tilde{n}k}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j&1&j\\
k&\alpha&n\end{array}\right)\right|_{j\to-(j+1)}=(-1)^{\alpha}w_{j}\epsilon^{j}_{\tilde{n}k}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j&1&j\\
k&\alpha&n\end{array}\right),

(181)

which results in

F_{0}(j,k)=F_{0}(-j-1,k).

(182)

Therefore

\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}=t^{m}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-\frac{t}{4}n^{2}+\frac{t}{4}}nF_{0}(\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{e^{n\eta}}{2\sinh(\eta)}

(183)

where
n\equiv 2j+1
.
Substituting the values of the 3-
j
and 2-
j
symbols, we have that

\displaystyle F_{0}(j,\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})=\delta(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i},0)\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(1+|\alpha_{i}|)^{1/2}}\right)\prod_{n=1}^{m}(\alpha_{n}j-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\alpha_{k})

(184)

Applying the Poisson summation formula to Eq. (
183
), we get

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}

(185)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\left(\sum_{i}\alpha_{i},0\right)t^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(1+|\alpha_{i}|)^{1/2}}e^{t/4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}x^{2}}x\prod_{n=1}^{m}(\alpha_{n}\frac{x-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\alpha_{k})\frac{e^{x\eta}}{2\sinh(\eta)}+O(t^{\infty}).

By the trick

\partial_{\eta}^{n}\frac{e^{x\eta}}{\sinh(\eta)}=\left.\left((x+\partial_{y})^{n}\frac{e^{x\eta}}{\sinh(y)}\right)\right|_{y\to\eta},

(186)

Eq. (
185
) can be finally simplified as

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}

(187)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\left(\sum_{i}\alpha_{i},0\right)t^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(1+|\alpha_{i}|)^{1/2}}e^{t/4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}xx\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-1}{2}x-\frac{\partial_{y}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}-\frac{\alpha_{k}}{2}\right)\frac{e^{-\frac{t}{4}x^{2}+x\eta}}{2\sinh(y)}\Big|_{y\to\eta}+O(t^{\infty})

The integrand is a polynomial of
x
multiplied by a Gaussian function
e^{-tx^{2}/4+x\eta}
. Thus let us integrate
x^{n}e^{-tx^{2}/2+x\eta}

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}xx^{n}e^{-\frac{t}{4}x^{2}+\eta x}=\frac{t}{e^{t/4}}\frac{\sinh(\eta)}{\eta}\langle 1\rangle\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\binom{n}{2k}\eta^{n-2k}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{k-n}(2k-1)!!

(188)

where
\lfloor y\rfloor
denotes the greatest integer less than or equal to
y
and Eq. (
29
) is substituted because we are concerned on the expectation value with respect to the normalized coherent states, i.e.
\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}/\langle 1\rangle
. The leading-order term of the RHS of Eq. (
188
) is
O(t^{-n+1})
with
n
the power of
x
in the integrand. Thus it is the highest-power term of
x
in the integrand of (
187
) that leads to the leading-order term of
\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}
, and the highest-power and second-highest-power terms of
x
lead to the next-to-leading order term of
\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}
, and so on.
Therefore, we finally get

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}

(189)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\left(\sum_{i}\alpha_{i},0\right)\langle 1\rangle\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(1+|\alpha_{i}|)^{1/2}}\Bigg\{\left(\prod_{k=1}^{m}(\alpha_{k}-1)\right)\eta^{m}+\left(\prod_{k=1}^{m}(\alpha_{k}-1)\right)\frac{(m+1)m}{2}\eta^{m-2}\frac{t}{2}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{i\neq k}(\alpha_{i}-1)\right)\left(\frac{\coth(\eta)+\alpha_{k}}{2}+\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{k-1}\right)\eta^{m-1}t+\cdots\Bigg\}.

D.2
for the case with
\iota\neq 0

Now let us consider the operator

\hat{F}_{\iota ab}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{n}}=\hat{p}_{s}^{\alpha_{1}}(e)\cdots\hat{p}_{s}^{\alpha_{m}}(e)D^{\iota}_{ab}(h_{e}),\ \iota\neq 0.

(190)

By using (
170
) and (
168
), it can be obtained that

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}_{\iota ab}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}=\sum_{jj^{\prime}}d_{j}e^{-\frac{t}{2}(j(j+1)+j^{\prime}(j^{\prime}+1))}d_{j^{\prime}}(-tw_{j^{\prime}})^{m}\sum_{kk^{\prime}}e^{kz_{e}+k^{\prime}\overline{z_{e}}}\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}k_{1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{1}&\alpha_{1}&n_{1}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{1}k_{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{2}&\alpha_{2}&n_{2}\end{array}\right)\cdots

(191)

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{m-1}&\alpha_{m-1}&n_{m-1}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{m-1}k_{m}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{m}&\alpha_{m}&n_{m}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{m}k_{m+1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k_{m+1}&a^{\prime}&k\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{\iota}_{aa^{\prime}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k^{\prime}&b&k^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j}_{k^{\prime\prime}k}

Because the products of these
3j
-symbols vanish unless
