, the last equation can be simplified as

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}\rangle_{z_{e}}=t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{t}{2}(j(j+1)+j^{\prime}(j^{\prime}+1))}F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh((2j^{\prime}+1)\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

(192)

where
F_{\iota}
is given by

\displaystyle F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}):=

\displaystyle d_{j}d_{j^{\prime}}(-w_{j^{\prime}})^{m}\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}k_{1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{1}&\alpha_{1}&n_{1}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{1}k_{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{2}&\alpha_{2}&n_{2}\end{array}\right)\cdots\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{m-1}&\alpha_{m-1}&n_{m-1}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{m-1}k_{m}}

(193)

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{m}&\alpha_{m}&n_{m}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{m}k_{m+1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k_{m+1}&a^{\prime}&k^{\prime}+b\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{\iota}_{aa^{\prime}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k^{\prime}&b&k^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j}_{k^{\prime\prime}\,k^{\prime}+b}.

The possible values of
j
are
|j^{\prime}-\iota|,\ |j^{\prime}-\iota|+1,\ \cdots,\ j^{\prime}+\iota
. By applying Eqs. (
147
) and (
164
) again, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{m}k_{m+1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k_{m+1}&a^{\prime}&k^{\prime}+b\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k^{\prime}&b&k^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j}_{k^{\prime\prime}\,k^{\prime}+b}\Bigg|_{j=j^{\prime}-\Delta}

(194)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{b-a+1}\left[\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{m}k_{m+1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k_{m+1}&a^{\prime}&k^{\prime}+b\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k^{\prime}&b&k^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j}_{k^{\prime\prime}\,k^{\prime}+b}\Bigg|_{j=j^{\prime}+\Delta}\right]_{j^{\prime}\to-j^{\prime}-1}.

Together with Eq. (
181
), it leads to

F_{\iota}(j^{\prime}+\Delta,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})=-F_{\iota}(j^{\prime}-\Delta,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})\Big|_{j^{\prime}\to-j^{\prime}-1}

(195)

where we use
F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},k)\propto\delta(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b,0)
.
Thus for
j^{\prime}=j\pm d
with
d>0
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{2j^{\prime}+d\geq\iota}f(d_{j^{\prime}}^{2})F_{\iota}(j^{\prime}+d,j^{\prime},\frac{\partial\eta}{2})\frac{\sinh(d_{j^{\prime}}\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}+\sum_{2j^{\prime}-d\geq\iota}f(d_{j^{\prime}}^{2})F_{\iota}(j^{\prime}-d,j^{\prime},\frac{\partial\eta}{2})\frac{\sinh(d_{j^{\prime}}\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{-2j^{\prime}-2+d\geq\iota}f(d_{j^{\prime}}^{2})F_{\iota}(-j^{\prime}-1+d,-j^{\prime}-1,\frac{\partial\eta}{2})\frac{\sinh(-d_{j^{\prime}}\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}+\sum_{2j^{\prime}-d\geq\iota}f(d_{j^{\prime}}^{2})F_{\iota}(j^{\prime}-d,j^{\prime},\frac{\partial\eta}{2})\frac{\sinh(d_{j^{\prime}}\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}\\
n\notin[d-\iota,d+\iota]\end{subarray}}f(d_{j^{\prime}}^{2})F_{\iota}(\frac{n-1}{2}-d,\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}.

Here the conditions
2j^{\prime}\pm d\geq\iota
are from the the triangle condition i.e.
j^{\prime}+j\geq\iota
, of the
3j
-symbol. The second equality is because of the replacement
j^{\prime}\to-j^{\prime}-1
in the first summation,
d_{j^{\prime}}=2j^{\prime}+1\equiv n
and
f
is an arbitrary function.
Therefore,

\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}\rangle_{z_{e}}=t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq d\leq\iota\\
d+\iota\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{2-\delta(d,0)}{2}e^{-\frac{t}{4}\left(2d^{2}-1\right)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}\\
n\notin[d-\iota,d+\iota]\end{subarray}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}t\left(n^{2}-2dn\right)}F_{\iota}(\frac{n-1}{2}-d,\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

(196)

To calculate
F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
, we notice that

\displaystyle-w_{j^{\prime}}\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{i-1}k_{i}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&1&j^{\prime}\\
k_{i}&\alpha_{i}&n_{i}\end{array}\right)=\delta(k_{i}+\alpha_{i}+n_{i},0)\left(\alpha_{i}j^{\prime}-n_{i}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\left(j^{\prime}-n_{i-1}\right)!\left(n_{i-1}+j^{\prime}\right)!}{\left(1-\alpha_{i}\right)!\left(\alpha_{i}+1\right)!\left(j^{\prime}-n_{i}\right)!\left(n_{i}+j^{\prime}\right)!}}.

(197)

Moreover, by applying Eq. (
147
) and the expression of the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients (Eq. (5), Section 8.2.1 in
varshalovich1988quantum
)
, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon^{j^{\prime}}_{n_{m}k_{m+1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k_{m+1}&a^{\prime}&k\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}j^{\prime}&\iota&j\\
k^{\prime}&b&k^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\epsilon^{j}_{k^{\prime\prime}\,k}=(-1)^{j^{\prime}-\iota-j+a^{\prime}+b}\delta_{a^{\prime}+k,n_{m}}\delta_{k^{\prime}+b,k}\times

(198)

\displaystyle\frac{(j+\iota-j^{\prime})!(j-\iota+j^{\prime})!(-j+\iota+j^{\prime})!}{(j+\iota+j^{\prime}+1)!}\left(\frac{(j^{\prime}+n_{m})!(j^{\prime}-n_{m})!}{(j^{\prime}+k^{\prime})!(j^{\prime}-k^{\prime})!(\iota+a^{\prime})!(\iota-a^{\prime})!(\iota+b)!(\iota-b)!}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\sum_{z}\frac{(-1)^{z}(j^{\prime}+\iota+k-z)!(j-k+z)!}{z!(j^{\prime}-j+\iota-z)!(j^{\prime}+k+a^{\prime}-z)!(j-\iota-k-a^{\prime}+z)!}\sum_{z}\frac{(-1)^{z}(j+\iota+k^{\prime}-z)!(j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+z)!}{z!(j-j^{\prime}+\iota-z)!(j+k-z)!(j^{\prime}-\iota-k+z)!}.

Eqs. (
197
) and (
198
) lead to

\displaystyle F_{\iota}(j^{\prime}-d,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\delta(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b,0)\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(1+|\alpha_{i}|)^{1/2}}\left(\frac{1}{(\iota+a)!(\iota-a)!(\iota+b)!(\iota-b)!}\right)^{1/2}

(199)

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{d-2a+b}(2j^{\prime}+1)(2j^{\prime}-2d+1)}{(2j^{\prime}-d+\iota+1)_{2\iota+1}}\prod_{n=1}^{m}(\alpha_{i}j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i})

\displaystyle\sum_{z}\frac{(-1)^{z}(d+\iota)_{z}(j^{\prime}+k^{\prime}+b-z+\iota)_{\iota+a}(j^{\prime}-d-k^{\prime}-b+z)_{\iota-a}}{z!}

\displaystyle\sum_{z}\frac{(-1)^{z}(\iota-d)_{z}(j^{\prime}-d+k^{\prime}-z+\iota)_{\iota-b}(j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+z)_{\iota+b}}{z!}

where
(x)_{n}:=x(x-1)\cdots(x-n+1)
is the falling factorial.

There is subtle issue on the summation over
k
and
k^{\prime}
in Eq. (
191
). In order to obtain the factor
\sinh((2j^{\prime}+1)\eta)/\sinh(\eta)
in (
192
), one has to consider a summation of
e^{2k^{\prime}\eta}
over all values of
k^{\prime}
in
[-j^{\prime},j^{\prime}]
.
However, except the constraint of
|k^{\prime}|\leq j^{\prime}
for
k^{\prime}
, there exists another condition of
|k^{\prime}+b|=|k|\leq j
implied by
k=k^{\prime}+b
, which seemingly narrows the range of
k^{\prime}
.
In order to preserve the range of
k^{\prime}
, one can always “artificially engineer”
F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
such that it vanishes for
|k^{\prime}+b|>j
, which has actually been done by the triangle inequality of
3j
-symbols in the RHS Eq. (
193
). Thus the subtlety is now encoded in the condition that
F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
vanishes for
|k^{\prime}+b|>j
. We need to verify this for the algebraic expression of
F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
in the RHS of Eq. (
199
). To check it, assume
k^{\prime}+b=j+\delta=j^{\prime}-d+\delta
with
0<\delta\leq d+b
. Then
(j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+z)_{\iota+b}
, in the second summand over
z
, vanishes because

j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+z\geq d+b-\delta\geq 0,\ j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+z-(\iota+b)+1\leq\delta<0

(200)

where
0\leq z\leq\iota-d
is applied.
Therefore, we conclude that the RHS of Eq.(
199
) vanishes for
j-b<k^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}
.
Similarly, it can be checked that
(j^{\prime}-d+k^{\prime}-z+\iota)_{\iota-b}
vanishes, indicating the vanishing of the RHS of Eq.(
199
) for
-j^{\prime}\leq k^{\prime}+b<-j-b
.
We thus claim that the RHS of Eq.(
199
) vanishes for
|k^{\prime}+b|>j
, which indicates that the expression of the RHS of Eq.(
199
) can be analytically extended to give
F_{\iota}(j,j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
for the full range of
k^{\prime}
, i.e.
|k^{\prime}|\leq j
.

Replacing
j^{\prime}
and
k^{\prime}
in Eq. (
199
) by
\frac{n-1}{2}
and
\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}
respectively, we have

\displaystyle F_{\iota}(\frac{n-1}{2}-d,\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})=

\displaystyle\delta(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b,0)\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(1+|\alpha_{i}|)^{1/2}}\left(\frac{1}{(\iota+a)!(\iota-a)!(\iota+b)!(\iota-b)!}\right)^{1/2}

(201)

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{d-2a+b}n(n-2d)}{(n-d+\iota)_{2\iota+1}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}(\alpha_{i}\frac{n-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i})

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0}^{\iota+d}\frac{(-1)^{z}(\iota+d)_{z}(\frac{n-1}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b-z+\iota)_{\iota+a}(\frac{n-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}-d-b+z)_{\iota-a}}{z!}

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0}^{\iota-d}\frac{(-1)^{z}(\iota-d)_{z}(\frac{n-1}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}-d-z+\iota)_{\iota-b}(\frac{n-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+z)_{\iota+b}}{z!}.

In order to apply the Poisson summation formula to (
196
) to calculate the summation over
n
, we need to extend the summation to entire
\mathbb{Z}
, while Eq. (
196
) sums
n
over
\mathbb{Z}\setminus[d-\iota,d+\iota]
. However, because of the term
(n-d+\iota)_{2\iota+1}
in the denominator of
F_{\iota}
, this extension is not trivial. We need to prove that
F_{\iota}(\frac{n-1}{2}-d,\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}
is well-defined at the points where
F_{\iota}
itself does not.
Once it is proven, we have

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}\rangle_{z_{e}}=t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq d\leq\iota\\
d+\iota\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{2-\delta(d,0)}{2}e^{-\frac{t}{4}\left(2d^{2}-1\right)}\int{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{1}{4}t\left(x^{2}-2dx\right)}F_{\iota}(\frac{x-1}{2}-d,\frac{x-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

(202)

\displaystyle-\sum_{n\in[d-\iota,d+\iota]\cap\mathbb{Z}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}t\left(n^{2}-2dn\right)}F_{\iota}(\frac{n-1}{2}-d,\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}+O(t^{\infty}).

Moreover, the integrals in the last equation usually produce a factor
e^{\eta^{2}/t}
. Thus the second term given by the summation over
n\in[d-\iota,d+\iota]\cap\mathbb{Z}
decays exponentially as
t\to 0
after normalization. Therefore, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}\rangle_{z_{e}}=t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq d\leq\iota\\
d+\iota\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{2-\delta(d,0)}{2}e^{-\frac{t}{4}\left(2d^{2}-1\right)}\int{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{1}{4}t\left(x^{2}-2dx\right)}F_{\iota}(\frac{x-1}{2}-d,\frac{x-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}+O(t^{\infty}).

(203)

In order to show that
F_{\iota}(\frac{n-1}{2}-d,\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}
is well-defined at the poles of
F_{\iota}
, we have checked that
F_{\iota}(\frac{n-1}{2}-d,\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2})
for at least
\iota\leq 20
can be simplified as summation of terms taking the form
7
7

7

Here, we conjecture that this statement is true for all
\iota
.

\frac{1}{x-n}f(x,\partial_{\eta})\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

where
f(x,\partial_{\eta})
, a polynomial of
x
and
\partial_{\eta}
, satisfies that
f(n,\cdot)
takes
\partial_{\eta}=2k
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[-\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{n-1}{2}]
as its zeros. Then, the following theorem will be helpful.

Theorem D.1
.

Let
f(x,k)
be a polynomials of
x
and
k
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
be some integer. Let

h(x):=\frac{1}{x-n}f(x,\partial_{\eta})\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}.

(204)

Then
h(n):=\lim_{x\to n}h(x)
is well-defined, i.e.,
n
is a removable singularity of
h(x)
, provided that for
x=n

f(x,\partial_{\eta})
satisfies

f(n,\partial_{\eta})=g(n,\partial_{\eta})\left(\prod_{k=-\frac{n-1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\left(\partial_{\eta}+2k\right)\right),

(205)

with some polynomial
g(n,\partial_{\eta})
of
n
and
\partial_{\eta}
.
Moreover, if (
205
) holds,
h(x)
will take the form

h(x)=g(x,\partial_{\eta})\left(\tilde{g}_{0}(x,\eta)\frac{\sinh((x-n)\eta)}{x-n}+\sum_{l\geq 1}g_{l}(x,\eta)(x-n)^{l}\right).

(206)

Proof.

We will prove that

\left(\prod_{k=-\frac{n-1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\left(\partial_{\eta}+2k\right)\right)\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}=\sum_{l}g_{l}(x,\eta)(x-n)^{l}

(207)

with
g_{l}
some function of
x
and
\eta
, and
g_{0}
taking the form

g_{0}(x,\eta)=\tilde{g}_{0}(x,\eta)\sinh((x-n)\eta).

(208)

Let
y=x-n
. Then we have

\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}=\frac{1}{\sinh(\eta)}\left(\cosh(y\eta)\sinh(n\eta)+\sinh(y\eta)\cosh(n\eta)\right).

(209)

Substitute the above expression into the LHS of Eq. (
207
) and expand the result. One then expresses the LHS of Eq. (
207
) as a linear combination of terms taking the forms of
\partial_{\eta}^{l}(h_{1}(\eta)\sinh(y\eta))
and
\partial_{\eta}^{l}(h_{2}(\eta)\cosh(y\eta))
, where
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are some arbitrary functions. Expanding the action of the differential operator by Leibniz’s rule, we obtain linear combinations of
q_{1}(\eta)\cosh(y\eta)y^{m}
and
q_{2}(\eta)\sinh(y\eta)y^{m}
, where
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
are some arbitrary functions. Thus, (
207
) is obtained.

To get
g_{0}
, one just act
\partial_{\eta}
on neither
\cosh(y\eta)
nor
\sinh(y\eta)
. Thus

g_{0}=\left(\prod_{k=-\frac{n-1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\left(\partial_{\eta}+2k\right)\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}\right)\cosh(y\eta)+\left(\prod_{k=-\frac{n-1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\left(\partial_{\eta}+2k\right)\frac{\cosh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}\right)\sinh(y\eta)

(210)

Since

\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}=\sum_{k=-j}^{j}e^{-2k\eta}
with
2j+1=n

and

(\partial_{\eta}+k)e^{-k\eta}=0
, we have

\prod_{k=-\frac{n-1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\left(\partial_{\eta}+2k\right)\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}=0.

(211)

Therefore

g_{0}=\left(\prod_{k=-\frac{n-1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\left(\partial_{\eta}+2k\right)\frac{\cosh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}\right)\sinh(y\eta)

(212)

∎

Appendix E
\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}\rangle_{z_{e}}
for the case of
\iota=1

By setting
\iota=1
in (
196
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}_{1ab}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}

(213)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)\frac{(-1)^{a}}{2}t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1+|a|)(1+|b|)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}\\
n\notin[-1,1]\end{subarray}}e^{-\frac{t}{4}(n^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(\alpha_{k}\frac{n-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\frac{n\left(a\frac{n-1}{2}+b+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}\right)\left(b\frac{n-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}\right)}{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}}\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)(-1)^{b-a}t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1+|a|)(1+|b|)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}\\
n\notin[-2,0]\end{subarray}}e^{-\frac{t}{4}(n+1)^{2}}

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(-\alpha_{k}\frac{n+1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\frac{\left(\frac{n+1}{2}-(1-|b|-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}\right)\left(\frac{n+1}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right)}{\frac{n+1}{2}}\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}.

where in the second term we replaced
n
by
-n
for the further convenience. It is easy to check that Theorem
D.1
can be applied to extend the summation in the last equation to all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}_{1ab}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}

(214)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{(-1)^{a}}{2}\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|a|}\sqrt{1+|b|}}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(x^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(\alpha_{k}\frac{x-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(a\frac{x-1}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b\right)\frac{2x\left(b(x-1)-\partial_{\eta}\right)}{x^{2}-1}\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{b-a}\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|a|}\sqrt{1+|b|}}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}xe^{-\frac{t}{4}(x+1)^{2}}

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(-\alpha_{k}\frac{n+1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{n+1}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right)\frac{\left(x+1-(1-|b|-b)\partial_{\eta}\right)}{x+1}\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle+O(t^{\infty}).

To show the algorithms to compute Eq. (
214
),
m\neq 0
will be assumed without the loss of generality. For the case of
m=0
, the algorithm can be applied very similarly.

To begin with, we will rewrite Eq. (
214
) as

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}_{1ab}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}

(215)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{(-1)^{a}}{2}\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|a|}\sqrt{1+|b|}}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(x^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\alpha_{k}\frac{x-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(a\frac{x-1}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b\right)x\left(b-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x-1}\right)\left(\alpha_{1}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x+1}\right)\frac{\sinh(n\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{b-a}\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|a|}\sqrt{1+|b|}}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(x+1)^{2}}

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(-\alpha_{k}\frac{x+1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{k}\right)\left(\frac{x+1}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right)\left(1-(1-|b|-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x+1}\right)\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}.

Then for the first integral, it is

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(x^{2}-1)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\alpha_{k}\frac{x-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(a\frac{x-1}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b\right)x\left(b-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x-1}\right)\left(\alpha_{1}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x+1}\right)\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}.

(216)

Because

\displaystyle\left(b-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x-1}\right)\left(\alpha_{1}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x+1}\right)\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

(217)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg(b\alpha_{1}\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}-\alpha_{1}\left(\frac{\cosh(\eta x)}{\sinh(\eta)}-\frac{\sinh(\eta(x-1))}{\sinh^{2}(\eta)(x-1)}\right)-b\left(\frac{\cosh(\eta x)}{\sinh(\eta)}-\frac{\sinh(\eta(x+1))}{\sinh^{2}(\eta)(x+1)}\right)+

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sinh(\eta x)}{\sinh(\eta)}+\frac{\cosh(\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)^{3}}\frac{\sinh(\eta(x-1))}{x-1}-\frac{\cosh(\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)^{3}}\frac{\sinh((x+1)\eta)}{x+1}\right)\Bigg),

I_{1}
can be deduced further as the following

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(x^{2}-1)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\alpha_{k}\frac{x-1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(a\frac{x-1}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b\right)x\frac{(b\alpha_{1}+1)\sinh(x\eta)-(\alpha_{1}+b)\cosh(\eta x)}{\sinh(\eta)}

(218)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}+2y)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\alpha_{k}\frac{y}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(a\frac{y}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b\right)(y+1)\frac{\alpha_{1}\sinh(\eta)+\cosh(\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)^{3}}\frac{\sinh(\eta y)}{y}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}-2y)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\alpha_{k}\frac{y}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(a\frac{y}{2}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b-a\right)(y-1)\frac{\cosh(\eta)-b\sinh(\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)^{3}}\frac{\sinh(y\eta)}{y}.

Then by using the trick

\partial_{\eta}^{n}e^{\pm x\eta}f(\eta)=\left.(\pm x+\partial_{z})^{n}e^{\pm x\eta}f(z)\right|_{z\to\eta},

(219)

we have

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(x^{2}-1)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-s}{2}x-\frac{\alpha_{k}+\partial_{z}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{a+s}{2}x+\frac{-a+\partial_{z}}{2}+b\right)

(220)

\displaystyle x\frac{(-(\alpha_{1}+b)+s(b\alpha_{1}+1))e^{sx}}{2\sinh(z)}\Big|_{z\to\eta}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}+2y)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-s}{2}y-\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{a+s}{2}y+\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+b\right)

\displaystyle(y+1)\frac{se^{s\eta y}}{2y}\frac{\alpha_{1}\sinh(z)+\cosh(z)}{\sinh(z)^{3}}\Big|_{z\to\eta}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}-2y)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-s}{2}y-\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{a+s}{2}y+\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+b-a\right)

\displaystyle(y-1)\frac{se^{s\eta y}}{y}\frac{\cosh(z)-b\sinh(z)}{\sinh(z)^{3}}\Big|_{z\to\eta}

By defining

\displaystyle C_{1}=

\displaystyle\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+b\right)\frac{\alpha_{1}\sinh(\eta)+\cosh(\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)^{3}}

(221)

\displaystyle C_{2}=

\displaystyle-\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\alpha_{k}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+b-a\right)\frac{\cosh(\eta)-b\sinh(\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)^{3}}

I_{1}
can be finally simplified as

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}x\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(x^{2}-1)}\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-s}{2}x-\frac{\alpha_{k}+\partial_{z}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{a+s}{2}x+\frac{-a+\partial_{z}}{2}+b\right)

(222)

\displaystyle x\frac{(-(\alpha_{1}+b)+s(b\alpha_{1}+1))e^{sx}}{2\sinh(z)}\Big|_{z\to\eta}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}+2y)}\Bigg(\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-s}{2}y-\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{a+s}{2}y+\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+b\right)

\displaystyle(y+1)\frac{\alpha_{1}\sinh(z)+\cosh(z)}{\sinh(z)^{3}}\Big|_{z\to\eta}-C_{1}\Bigg)\frac{se^{s\eta y}}{2y}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}-2y)}\Bigg(\prod_{k=2}^{m}\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-s}{2}y-\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{a+s}{2}y+\frac{\partial_{z}}{2}+b-a\right)

\displaystyle(y-1)\frac{\cosh(z)-b\sinh(z)}{\sinh(z)^{3}}\Big|_{z\to\eta}-C_{2}\Bigg)\frac{se^{s\eta y}}{y}

\displaystyle+C_{1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}+2y)}\frac{\sinh(\eta y)}{y}{\rm d}y-C_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}-2y)}\frac{\sinh(\eta y)}{y}{\rm d}y.

In this expression, the integrands of first three terms involve only polynomials of the integral variables, which can be computed by using the same procedure as that to compute
\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\frac{1}{2}ab}\rangle
. For the last two terms, by using the formula

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}ye^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}+by)}\frac{\sinh(y\eta)}{y}=\frac{1}{2}\pi\left(\text{erfi}\left(\frac{1+\frac{tb}{4\eta}}{2\sqrt{\frac{t}{4\eta^{2}}}}\right)+\text{erfi}\left(\frac{1-\frac{tb}{4\eta}}{2\sqrt{\frac{t}{4\eta^{2}}}}\right)\right),

(223)

and

\displaystyle\text{erfi}\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)=e^{\frac{1}{x^{2}}}\frac{x}{\pi}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+n)x^{2n},

(224)

a straightforward calculation can be performed as the following

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\,e^{-\frac{t}{4}(y^{2}\pm 2y)}\frac{\sinh(y\eta)}{y}=\langle 1\rangle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{\sinh(\eta)}{\eta^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+n)\frac{2^{n}}{\eta^{n+m}}\binom{n+m}{m-n}\Bigg\{e^{-\eta}+e^{\eta}\left(-1\right)^{m-n}\Bigg\}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{m+2},

(225)

where Eq. (
29
) is substitute because we are concerned about the expectation value with respect to the normalized coherent state, i.e.
\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}/\langle 1\rangle
.
This complete our computation for
I_{1}
.

Secondly,
I_{2}
can be simplified as the following

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}xe^{-\frac{t}{4}(x+1)^{2}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(-\alpha_{k}\frac{x+1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{x+1}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right)\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

(226)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}xe^{-\frac{t}{4}(x+1)^{2}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left(-\alpha_{k}\frac{x+1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right)\left(\frac{x+1}{2}+(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right)\frac{(1-|b|-b)\partial_{\eta}}{x+1}\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}.

Because

\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{x+1}\frac{\sinh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}=\frac{1}{\sinh(\eta)^{2}}\left(\sinh(\eta)\cosh(x\eta)-\frac{\sinh((x+1)\eta)}{x+1}\right)

(227)

we have

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}xe^{-\frac{t}{4}(x+1)^{2}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left[-\alpha_{k}\frac{x+1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right]\left[\frac{x+1}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right]\times

(228)

\displaystyle\frac{\sinh(x\eta)-(1-|b|-b)\cosh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}ye^{-\frac{t}{4}y^{2}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left[-\alpha_{k}\frac{y}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right]\left[\frac{y}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right]\frac{(1-|b|-b)}{\sinh(\eta)^{2}}\frac{\sinh(y\eta)}{y}.

By defining

C=\left(\alpha_{1}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}\right)\left(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}\right)\cdots\left((\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{m}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}\right)\left(|b|-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}\right)\frac{1-|b|-b}{\sinh(\eta)^{2}},

(229)

we obtain

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}xe^{-\frac{t}{4}(x+1)^{2}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left[-\alpha_{k}\frac{x+1}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{i}\right]\times

(230)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{x+1}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right]\frac{\sinh(x\eta)-(1-|b|-b)\cosh(x\eta)}{\sinh(\eta)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}ye^{-\frac{t}{4}y^{2}}\Bigg\{\prod_{k=1}^{m}\left[-\alpha_{k}\frac{y}{2}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\alpha_{k}\right]\left[\frac{y}{2}-(|b|-1-b)\frac{\partial_{\eta}}{2}+|b|\right]-C\Bigg\}\frac{(1-|b|-b)}{\sinh(\eta)^{2}}\frac{\sinh(y\eta)}{y}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}ye^{-\frac{t}{4}y^{2}}\frac{\sinh(y\eta)}{y}.

Similar as
I_{1}
, the first two integrals are computable because the integrands therein are just polynomials multiplied by the Gaussian functions. For the last term, we have

\displaystyle C\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}ye^{-\frac{t}{4}y^{2}}\frac{\sinh(y\eta)}{y}=C\mathrm{sgn}(\eta)\pi\text{erfi}\left(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\frac{t}{4\eta^{2}}}}\right)

(231)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle 1\rangle C\frac{t^{2}}{2\sqrt{\pi}e^{t/4}}\frac{\sinh(\eta)}{\eta^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+n)\left(\frac{t}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{n}.

where Eq. (
29
) is substitute because we are concerned about the expectation value with respect to the normalized coherent state, i.e.
\langle\hat{F}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}_{\iota ab}\rangle/\langle 1\rangle
.
Therefore,
I_{2}
is computable.

In summary, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\hat{F}_{1ab}^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}}\rangle_{z_{e}}=-\frac{(-1)^{a}}{2}\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|a|}\sqrt{1+|b|}}\right)I_{1}

(232)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-1)^{b-a}\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-a+b\right)t^{m}e^{bz_{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\alpha_{i}|}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|a|}\sqrt{1+|b|}}\right)I_{2}+O(t^{\infty})

where
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
can be computed by using the algorithm introduced above. Taking the operator
\hat{F}_{1ab}=D^{1}_{ab}(h_{e})
as an example, we can have

\langle\hat{F}_{1ab}\rangle_{z_{e}}/\langle 1\rangle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}e^{i\xi}-\left(\frac{e^{i\xi}\tanh\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{4}e^{i\xi}\right)t+O(t^{2}),&\ a=b=1,\\
1-\frac{\tanh\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)}{\eta}t+O(t^{2}),&\ a=b=0,\\
e^{-i\xi}-\left(\frac{e^{-i\xi}\tanh\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{4}e^{-i\xi}\right)t+O(t^{2}),&\ a=b=-1,\end{array}\right.

(233)

which is compatible with the corresponding result given in
Dapor:2017gdk
.

Appendix F
Mathematical supports for Sec.
IV

To begin with, we need to study the matrix element of the holonomy and flux. According to the results in
thiemann2001gaugeIII
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\hat{p}_{s}^{\alpha}|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle=i\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\psi_{{g_{e}^{\prime}}}\rangle\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\tau^{\alpha}g_{e}^{\prime}g_{e}^{\dagger})}{\sinh(\zeta_{e}^{(0)})}\left(-\zeta_{e}^{(0)}+(\coth(\zeta_{e}^{(0)})-\frac{1}{\zeta_{e}^{(0)}})\frac{t}{2}\right)+O(t^{\infty}).

(234)

where
\zeta_{e}^{(0)}
is given by
2\cosh(\zeta_{e}^{(0)})=\mathrm{tr}(g_{e}^{\prime}g_{e}^{\dagger})
. For
\hat{p}_{t}^{\alpha}(e)
, one has

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\hat{p}_{t}^{\alpha}|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle=i\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\psi_{{g_{e}^{\prime}}}\rangle\frac{\mathrm{tr}(g_{e}^{\dagger}g_{e}^{\prime}\tau^{\alpha})}{\sinh(\zeta_{e}^{(0)})}\left(\zeta_{e}^{(0)}-(\coth(\zeta_{e}^{(0)})-\frac{1}{\zeta_{e}^{(0)}})\frac{t}{2}\right)+O(t^{\infty}).

(235)

For the holonomy operator
D^{\iota}_{ab}(h_{e})
,
we have
thiemann2001gaugeIII

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{ab}(h_{e})|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle

(236)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle\left(\frac{2\mathrm{tr}(\tau^{k}g_{e}^{\prime}g_{e}^{\dagger})D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{ab}(\tau^{k}g_{e}^{\prime})}{\left(e^{\zeta_{e}^{(0)}}+1\right)^{2}\zeta_{e}^{(0)}}e^{\frac{\zeta_{e}^{(0)}}{2}}+\frac{D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{ab}(g_{e}^{\prime})}{2\zeta_{e}^{(0)}}e^{-\frac{\zeta_{e}^{(0)}}{2}}\right)e^{\frac{-t}{16}}\left(\left(e^{\zeta_{e}^{(0)}}+1\right)\zeta_{e}^{(0)}+(-e^{\zeta_{e}^{(0)}}+1)\frac{t}{4}\right)+O(t^{\infty}).

According to Eqs. (
234
), (
235
) and (
236
), the matrix elements of the fluxes and holomomies are of a form described below

\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\hat{O}_{i}|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle=\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle\left(E_{0}(g_{e},g_{e}^{\prime})+tE_{1}(g_{e},g_{e}^{\prime})+O(t^{\infty})\right).

(237)

Recalling Eq. (
56
), we are going to consider integrals containing
\frac{\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle}{\|\psi_{g_{e}}\|\|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\|}
. These integrals can be analyzed with the generalized stationary phase approximation guided by Hörmander’s theorem 7.7.5 in
hormander2015analysis
.

Theorem F.1
.

Let
K
be a compact subset in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, X be an open neighborhood of K, and k be a positive integer. If (1) the complex functions
u\in C^{2k}_{0}(K)
,
f\in C^{3k+1}(X)
and
\Im(f)\geq 0
in X, with
\Im(f)
being the imaginary part of
f
; (2) there is a unique point
x_{0}\in K
satisfying
\Im(f(x_{0}))=0
,
f^{\prime}(x_{0})=0
, and
\det(f^{\prime\prime}(x_{0}))\neq 0
(
f^{\prime\prime}
denotes the Hessian matrix),
f^{\prime}\neq 0
in
K\backslash\left\{x_{0}\right\}
then we have the following estimation:

\left|\int_{K}u(x)e^{i\lambda f(x)}dx-e^{i\lambda f(x_{0})}\left[\mathrm{det}\left(\dfrac{\lambda f^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})}{2\pi i}\right)\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{s=0}^{k-1}\left(\dfrac{1}{\lambda}\right)^{s}L_{s}u(x_{0})\right|\leq C\left(\dfrac{1}{\lambda}\right)^{k}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2k}\mathrm{sup}\left|D^{\alpha}u\right|.

(238)

Here the constant C is bounded when f stays in a bounded set in
C^{3k+1}(X)
. We have used the standard multi-index notation
\alpha=\langle\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{n}\rangle
and

D^{\alpha}=(-i)^{\alpha}\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}}{\partial x_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}...\partial x_{n}^{\alpha_{n}}},\quad\text{where}\quad|\alpha|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}

(239)

L_{s}u(x_{0})
denotes the following operation on u:

L_{s}u(x_{0})=i^{-s}\sum_{l-m=s}\sum_{2l\geq 3m}\frac{(-1)^{l}2^{-l}}{l!m!}\left[\sum_{a,b=1}^{n}H_{ab}^{-1}(x_{0})\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{a}\partial x_{b}}\right]^{l}\left(g_{x_{0}}^{m}u\right)\left(x_{0}\right),

(240)

where
H(x)=f^{\prime\prime}(x)
denotes the Hessian matrix and the function
g_{x_{0}}(x)
is given by

g_{x_{0}}(x)=f(x)-f(x_{0})-\frac{1}{2}H^{ab}\left(x_{0}\right)\left(x-x_{0}\right)_{a}\left(x-x_{0}\right)_{b}

(241)

such that
g_{x_{0}}\left(x_{0}\right)=g^{\prime}_{x_{0}}\left(x_{0}\right)=g^{\prime\prime}_{x_{0}}\left(x_{0}\right)=0
. For each s,
L_{s}
is a differential operator of order 2s acting on
u\left(x\right)
.

F.1
Analysis of integrals containing
\frac{\langle\psi_{g_{e}}|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\rangle}{\|\psi_{g_{e}}\|\|\psi_{g_{e}^{\prime}}\|}

Define

G(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)}):=\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\|\psi_{g^{(1)}}\|\|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\|}

(242)

with
g^{(k)}
parameterized by

g^{(k)}=e^{i\vec{p}^{(k)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{\vec{\theta}^{(k)}\cdot\vec{\tau}},\ k=1,2.

(243)

According to Eq. (
28
) and Eq. (
29
),
G(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})
reads

G(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})=\frac{\zeta\sqrt{\sinh(p^{(1)})\sinh(p^{(2)})}}{\sqrt{p^{(1)}p^{(2)}}\sinh(\zeta)}e^{-\frac{-2\zeta^{2}+(p^{(1)})^{2}+(p^{(2)})^{2}}{2t}}

(244)

where
p^{(i)}=\sqrt{\vec{p}^{(i)}\cdot\vec{p}^{(i)}}
and
\zeta
is given by

2\cosh(\zeta)=\mathrm{tr}(g_{1}^{\dagger}g_{2}).

(245)

Denote

S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)}):=-2\zeta^{2}+(p^{(1)})^{2}+(p^{(2)})^{2}.

We first claim that

Lemma F.1
.

\Re(S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)}))
, the real part of
S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})
, is non-negative and vanishes iff
\vec{p}^{(2)}=\vec{p}^{(1)}
and
\vec{\theta}^{(2)}=\vec{\theta}^{(1)}
.

To prove this lemma, let us introduce the following proposition which is given in
thiemann2001gaugeII
.

Proposition 3
.

Let
g=e^{i\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\tau}}
. Considering
\zeta=s+i\phi\in\mathbb{C}
with
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\phi\in[0,\pi]
determined by
\cosh(\zeta)=\mathrm{tr}(g)
, we have, with denoting
p:=\sqrt{\vec{p}\cdot\vec{p}}
,

\delta=\frac{p^{2}}{4}-s^{2}+\phi^{2}\geq 0

(246)

where the equality occurs iff
\theta:=\sqrt{\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\theta}}=0
.

Thanks to this proposition, we prove Lemma
F.1
as follows.

Proof of Lemma
F.1
.

g_{1}^{\dagger}g_{2}
can be decomposed as

g_{1}^{\dagger}g_{2}=e^{i\vec{x}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{\vec{y}\cdot\vec{\tau}}.

Denote
x=\sqrt{\vec{x}\cdot\vec{x}}
and
y=\sqrt{\vec{y}\cdot\vec{y}}
. Then

\Re(S(\vec{p},\vec{\theta},\vec{p},\vec{\theta}))=2\delta-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+(p^{(1)})^{2}+(p^{(2)})^{2}.

(247)

where
\delta:=x^{2}/4-\Re(\zeta)^{2}
is non-negative according to the proposition
3
. Thus we only need to prove that
-x^{2}/2+(p^{(1)})^{2}+(p^{(2)})^{2}\geq 0
.

By definition, we have

2\cosh(x)=\mathrm{tr}(g_{1}^{\dagger}g_{2}g_{2}^{\dagger}g_{1})
,
which leads to

2\cosh(x)=\mathrm{tr}(e^{2i\vec{p}^{(1)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{2i\vec{p}^{(2)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}).

(248)

Since
e^{i\vec{\mu}\cdot\vec{\tau}}=\cosh(\frac{\mu}{2})\mathbb{I}+2i\frac{\vec{\mu}\cdot\vec{\tau}}{\mu}\sinh(\frac{\mu}{2})
with
\mu=\sqrt{\vec{\mu}\cdot\vec{\mu}}
, we have

\displaystyle\cosh(x)=\frac{1-\beta}{2}\cosh(p^{(1)}-p^{(2)})+\frac{1+\beta}{2}\cosh(p^{(1)}+p^{(2)})\leq\cosh(p^{(1)}+p^{(2)})

(249)

where
\beta=\frac{\vec{p}^{(1)}\cdot\vec{p}^{(2)}}{p^{(1)}p^{(2)}}\in[-1,1]
and
\cosh(p^{(1)}+p^{(2)})\geq\cosh(p^{(1)}-p^{(2)})
is used. Moreover, because of
\sqrt{2(p^{(1)})^{2}+2(p^{(2)})^{2}}\geq p^{(1)}+p^{(2)}\geq 0
, it has

\cosh(\sqrt{2(p^{(1)})^{2}+2(p^{(2)})^{2}}\,)\geq\cosh(p^{(1)}+p^{(2)}).

(250)

Combining the results of (
249
) and (
250
), one finally have

-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+(p^{(1)})^{2}+(p^{(2)})^{2}\geq 0

(251)

where the equality occurs only if
\vec{p}^{(1)}=\vec{p}^{(2)}
.

In summary we have

\Re(S(\vec{p},\vec{\theta},\vec{p},\vec{\theta}))\geq 0

(252)

and
\Re(S(\vec{p},\vec{\theta},\vec{p},\vec{\theta}))=0
only if
\vec{p}^{(1)}=\vec{p}^{(2)}
and
\delta=0
which means
\vec{\theta}^{(1)}=\vec{\theta}^{(2)}
.
∎

It turns out below that the integrand of Eq. (
56
) consists of a Gaussian-like function

e^{-\frac{1}{2t}\left(S(\vec{p},\vec{\theta},\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)})+S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})+\cdots+S(\vec{p}^{(k)},\vec{\theta}^{(k)},\vec{p},\vec{\theta})\right)}.

(253)

Lemma
F.1
suggests us to do the stationary phase approximation analysis at
\vec{p}^{(i)}=\vec{p}
and
\vec{\theta}^{(i)}=\vec{\theta}
. Notice that
\vec{p}
and
\vec{\theta}
are given to parameterize
g
as
g:=e^{i\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\tau}}
,
and
g
labels the coherent state
|\psi_{g}\rangle
with respect to which the expectation value of
\hat{O}
is computed. Thus, rather than considering all values of
\vec{p}
and
\vec{\theta}
, it is sufficient to set

\displaystyle\vec{p}_{o}=(0,0,p),\ \vec{\theta}_{o}=(0,0,\theta)

(254)

according to (
33
). Denote

f_{k;\vec{p},\vec{\theta}}(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\cdots,\vec{p}^{(k)},\vec{\theta}^{(k)})\equiv S(\vec{p},\vec{\theta},\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)})+S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})+\cdots+S(\vec{p}^{(k)},\vec{\theta}^{(k)},\vec{p},\vec{\theta}),

(255)

we have the following result:

Theorem F.2
.

(i)

\Re(f_{k;\vec{p},\vec{\theta}})\geq 0
and the equality occurs only when all
g^{(i)}
coincide, namely
\vec{p}^{\,(i)}=\vec{p}
and
\vec{\theta}^{(i)}=\vec{\theta}
.

(ii)

At
\vec{p}^{(i)}=\vec{p}_{o}=(0,0,p)
and
\vec{\theta}^{(i)}=\vec{\theta}_{o}=(0,0,\theta)
, it has

\nabla_{\vec{p}^{(i)}}f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=0=\nabla_{\vec{\theta}^{(i)}}f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}},\ \forall i=1,\cdots,k.

(256)

(iii)

The Hessian matrix
f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
of
f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
at
\vec{p}^{(i)}=\vec{p}_{o}=(0,0,p)
and
\vec{\theta}^{(i)}=\vec{\theta}_{o}=(0,0,\theta)
is non-degenerate with the determinant

\det\left(f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\right)\Big|_{\vec{p}^{(i)}=\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}^{(i)}=\vec{\theta}_{o}}=\left(\frac{1024\sin^{4}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta^{4}}\right)^{k}

(257)

Proof.

The first statement is true by using Lemma
F.1
. For the second statement, let us consider

S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})=-2\zeta^{2}+(\vec{p}^{(1)})^{2}+(\vec{p}^{(2)})^{2}

(258)

where
\zeta
is given by

\cosh(\zeta)=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}(e^{\vec{\theta}^{(2)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{-\vec{\theta}^{(1)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{i\vec{p}^{(1)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{i\vec{p}^{(2)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}).

(259)

Then it has that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\cosh(\zeta)}{\partial p_{j}^{(1)}}\Bigg|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=\frac{\partial\cosh(\zeta)}{\partial p_{j}^{(2)}}\Bigg|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{j,3}}{2}\sinh(p)

(260)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\cosh(\zeta)}{\partial\theta_{j}^{(1)}}\Bigg|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=-\frac{\partial\cosh(\zeta)}{\partial\theta_{j}^{(2)}}\Bigg|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i\delta_{j,3}}{2}\sinh(p)

where the subscript
\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}
indicates to take values at
\vec{p}^{(1)}=\vec{p}_{o}=\vec{p}^{(2)}
and
\vec{\theta}^{(1)}=\vec{\theta}_{o}=\vec{\theta}^{(2)}
. According to Eq. (
260
), we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{p}^{(1)}}S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})\Big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=0=\nabla_{\vec{p}^{(2)}}S(\vec{p}^{(1)},\vec{\theta}^{(1)},\vec{p}^{(2)},\vec{\theta}^{(2)})\Big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}

(261)

and, thus,
\nabla_{\vec{p}^{(i)}}f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=0
for all
i=1,2,\cdots,k
. For
\nabla_{\vec{\theta}^{(i)}}f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
, the second equation in Eq. (
260
) gives us

\nabla_{\vec{\theta}^{(i)}}f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=\nabla_{\vec{\theta}^{(i)}}S(\vec{p}^{(i-1)},\vec{\theta}^{(i-1)},\vec{p}^{(i)},\vec{\theta}^{(i)})\Big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}+\nabla_{\vec{\theta}^{(i)}}S(\vec{p}^{(i)},\vec{\theta}^{(i)},\vec{p}^{(i+1)},\vec{\theta}^{(i+1)})\Big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=0,

(262)

which completes the proof of the second statement.

For the last statement, using the conclusion of the second statement, we can immediately get that

\frac{\partial f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}}{\partial x_{m}^{(i)}\partial y_{n}^{(j)}}\Bigg|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=0,\ |i-j|>1

(263)

where
x_{m}^{(i)}
and
y_{n}^{(j)}
represent
\vec{\theta}_{m}^{(i)}
or
\vec{p}_{m}^{(i)}
.
Therefore, if we order the arguments
\vec{p}^{(i)},\vec{\theta}^{(i)}
as

p^{(1)}_{1},p^{(1)}_{2},p^{(1)}_{3},\theta^{(1)}_{1},\theta^{(1)}_{2},\theta^{(1)}_{3},p^{(2)}_{1},p^{(2)}_{2},p^{(2)}_{3},\theta^{(2)}_{1},\theta^{(2)}_{2},\theta^{(2)}_{3},\cdots.

(264)

to arrange the matrix elements of
f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o})\equiv f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\Big|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
, the resulting matrix is block-tridiagonal matrix. Moreover, since all of the
p
-arguments, as well as the
\theta
-arguments, in
f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
are symmetric, we conclude that

\frac{\partial f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}}{\partial x_{m}^{(i)}\partial y_{l}^{(j)}}\Bigg|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}=\frac{\partial f_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}}{\partial x_{m}^{(i^{\prime})}\partial y_{l}^{(j^{\prime})}}\Bigg|_{\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}.

(265)

Consequently
f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o})
takes the form

f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}A&B&0&0&\cdots&0\\
B^{T}&A&B&0&\cdots&0\\
0&B^{T}&A&B&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&B^{T}&A&B\\
0&0&\cdots&0&B^{T}&A\end{array}\right)

(266)

where
A
and
B
are
6\times 6
matrix. The matrix
A
and
B
can be calculated by considering the case with
k=2
, which gives us

A=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\frac{4\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{p}&0&0&-\frac{4\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&-\frac{2\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&0\\
0&\frac{4\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{p}&0&\frac{2\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&-\frac{4\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&0\\
0&0&2&0&0&0\\
-\frac{4\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&\frac{2\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&0&-\frac{4p(\cos(\theta)-1)\coth(p)}{\theta^{2}}&0&0\\
-\frac{2\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&-\frac{4\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{\theta}&0&0&-\frac{4p(\cos(\theta)-1)\coth(p)}{\theta^{2}}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&2\\
\end{array}\right)

and

B=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}-\frac{2\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{p}&0&0&\frac{2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)+i\sin(\theta)}{\theta}&\frac{\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)+i(\cos(\theta)-1)}{\theta}&0\\
0&-\frac{2\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{p}&0&\frac{-i\cos(\theta)-\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)+i}{\theta}&\frac{2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)+i\sin(\theta)}{\theta}&0\\
0&0&-1&0&0&i\\
\frac{2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)-i\sin(\theta)}{\theta}&\frac{i\left(\cos(\theta)+i\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)-1\right)}{\theta}&0&\frac{2p(\cos(\theta)-1)\coth(p)}{\theta^{2}}&-\frac{2ip(\cos(\theta)-1)}{\theta^{2}}&0\\
\frac{-i\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)+i}{\theta}&\frac{2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)-i\sin(\theta)}{\theta}&0&\frac{2ip(\cos(\theta)-1)}{\theta^{2}}&\frac{2p(\cos(\theta)-1)\coth(p)}{\theta^{2}}&0\\
0&0&-i&0&0&-1\\
\end{array}\right).

With the expression of
A
and
B
, it can be verified that

BA^{-1}B^{T}=B^{T}A^{-1}B=0.

(267)

To calculate
\det(f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}))
, we define matrices of
\tilde{B}
,
\tilde{C}
and D of dimensions
6\times 6(k-1)
,
6(k-1)\times 6
and
6(k-1)\times 6(k-1)
respectively such that

f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&\tilde{B}\\
\tilde{C}&D\end{array}\right).

(268)

Then by using the property of the Schur complement, it has

\det(f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}))=\det(A)\det(D-\tilde{C}A^{-1}\tilde{B})=\det(A)\det(D)

(269)

where we used
\tilde{C}A^{-1}\tilde{B}=0
because of Eq. (
267
) and that
f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
is block-tridiagonal matrix. Because
D
is the Hessian matrix
f^{\prime\prime}_{k-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o})
, we finally have

\det(f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}(\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}))=\det(A)^{k}=\left(\frac{1024\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta}{2})}{\theta^{4}}\right)^{k}.

(270)

∎

By these results, the stationary phase approximation method introduced in Theorem
F.1
can be applied to calculate the integral (
56
).

Taking advantage of the above results, we now can come to the proof of Theorem
IV.1
.

F.2
proof of Theorem
IV.1

As in Eq. (
56
), it has

\frac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\hat{O}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}=\int\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\frac{2}{\pi t^{3}}{\rm d}\mu_{H}(u^{(j)}_{e}){\rm d}^{3}\vec{p}_{e}^{(j)}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}|\hat{O}_{i}|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\rangle}{\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}\|\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\|}

(271)

where we denoted
|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(0)}}\rangle=|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(k)}}\rangle:=|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle
, applied Eq. (
31
) and used the decomposition

g_{e}^{(i)}=e^{i\vec{p}^{(i)}_{e}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{\vec{\theta}^{(i)}_{e}\cdot\vec{\tau}}=e^{i\vec{p}^{(i)}_{e}\cdot\vec{\tau}}u_{e}^{(i)}.

(272)

By the assumption, we have

\frac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}|\hat{O}_{i}|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\rangle}{\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}\|\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\|}=\frac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\rangle}{\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}\|\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\|}\left(E_{0}^{(i)}(\vec{g}^{(i-1)},\vec{g}^{(i)})+tE_{1}^{(i)}(\vec{g}^{(i-1)},\vec{g}^{(i)})+O(t^{\infty})\right).

(273)

Thus Eq. (
271
) takes the form

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\hat{O}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}=\int\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\frac{2}{\pi t^{3}}{\rm d}\mu_{H}(u^{(j)}_{e}){\rm d}^{3}\vec{p}_{e}^{(j)}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\rangle}{\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i-1)}}\|\|\Psi_{\vec{g}^{(i)}}\|}\prod_{l=1}^{k}E^{(l)}(\vec{g}^{(l-1)},\vec{g}^{(l)})

(274)

with
P(\{\vec{g}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k})
denoting the function

E^{(l)}(\vec{g}^{(l-1)},\vec{g}^{(l)}):=\left(E_{0}^{(l)}(\vec{g}^{(l-1)},\vec{g}^{(l)})+tE_{1}^{(l)}(\vec{g}^{(l-1)},\vec{g}^{(l)})+O(t^{\infty})\right).

(275)

Eq. (
274
) can be analyzed with the stationary phase approximation
according to Theorem
F.2
.
It should be noticed that the Haar measure
{\rm d}\mu_{H}
can be expressed as

{\rm d}\mu_{H}(u)=\frac{\sin^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\theta}}\right)}{4\pi^{2}\,(\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\theta})}{\rm d}^{3}\vec{\theta}

(276)

where
u\in\mathrm{SU}(2)
is coordinatized as
u=e^{\vec{\theta}\cdot\vec{\tau}}
and
{\rm d}^{3}\vec{\theta}
is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Substituting the last equation into Eq. (
274
) and applying Eq. (
238
) as well as Eq. (
257
), we finally obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\hat{O}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}=\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\left(\frac{\theta_{e}^{2}}{\sin^{2}(\theta_{e}/2)}\right)^{k-1}\,\sum_{s=0}^{l-1}\left(2t\right)^{s}\mathfrak{D}^{(s)}\Big|_{g_{e}^{(k)}=e^{iw_{e}\tau_{3}},\forall k,e}+O(t^{l})

(277)

where
\mathfrak{D}^{(s)}
takes the form

\mathfrak{D}^{(s)}=(-1)^{s}\sum_{l-j=s}\sum_{2l\geq 3j}\frac{(-1)^{l}2^{-l}}{l!j!}\left[\sum_{a,b=1}^{n}H_{ab}^{-1}(x_{0})\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{a}\partial x_{b}}\right]^{l}\left(G^{(j)}\prod_{i=1}^{k}E^{(i)}\right)\Bigg|_{g_{e}^{(l)}=e^{iw_{e}\tau_{3}},\forall l,e}

(278)

with
G^{(j)}
being defined as the following,

G^{(j)}\left(\{\vec{g}^{(i)}\}_{i=k}^{k-1}\right)=\left(\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\prod_{n=1}^{k-1}\frac{\sin^{2}(|\vec{\theta}_{e}^{(n)}|/2)}{|\vec{\theta}_{e}^{(n)}|^{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\prod_{n=0}^{k-1}\frac{\zeta_{e}^{(n,n+1)}\sqrt{\sinh(p_{e}^{(n)})\sinh(p_{e}^{(n+1)})}}{\sqrt{p_{e}^{(n)}p_{e}^{(n+1)}}\sinh(\zeta_{e}^{(n,n+1)})}\right)g_{x_{0}}(\{\vec{g}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k-1})^{j}.

(279)

Here
g_{x_{0}}
is some function defined by applying (
241
) to the current case.

If the leading order term of
\mathfrak{D}^{(s)}
is claimed to be
O(t^{d_{s}})
, then each term in the summation over
s
of Eq. (
277
) is
O(t^{s+d_{s}})
.
Moreover, for each
l
in Eq. (
278
), the derivative acting on
G^{(j)}\prod E^{(i)}
is of order
2l
in total. Because of the properties given by Eq.(
241
), the non-vanishing result appears when there are at least
3j
derivatives acting on
G^{(j)}
, which indicates that the order of derivative that acts on the term
\prod E^{(i)}
in Eq. (
278
) is
2l-3j
. Because
2l-3j=s
and
l=s+j
, there are at most
2s-j
derivatives acting on
\prod E^{(i)}
. Further, since
j\geq 0
, the maximum order of derivative acting on the term
\prod E^{(i)}
is
2s
, which only occurs for
l=s
.
Then, let us count the leading order of
\mathfrak{D}^{(s)}
for a given
s
.
According to the expression of
\mathfrak{D}^{(s)}
, once it is evaluated at the critical point given by
g_{e}^{(k)}=e^{i\omega_{e}\tau_{3}}
, those
E^{(m)}
contributed by operators
\hat{O}_{m}
satisfying (
61
) will inevitably increase the power of its leading order term if they are not acted by any derivative operators.
For a fixed
s
there are at least
n_{s}
of these “non-acted” terms with

n_{s}=\frac{(N_{0}-2s)+|N_{0}-2s|}{2}.

(280)

Therefore,
\dfrac{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\hat{O}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}{\langle\Psi_{\vec{w}}|\Psi_{\vec{w}}\rangle}
is of order of
t^{n}
with

n\geq\min\limits_{s\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}(s+n_{s})=\lfloor\frac{N_{0}+1}{2}\rfloor.

(281)

F.3
proof of Theorem
IV.2

At first,
denote
\hat{p}^{0}(e)
,
D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})
and
D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})
by
\hat{O}_{a}^{\rm d}
with
a=1,2,3
respectively, and
p^{\pm 1}(e)
,
D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})
or
D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})
by
\hat{O}_{a}^{\rm nd}
with
a=1,2,3,4
respectively. According to (
234
), (
235
) and (
236
), the matrix elements of
\hat{O}_{a}^{i}
take the form

\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{O}_{a}^{i}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle=\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle E^{i}_{a;0}(g^{(1)},g^{(2)})+O(t),\ \forall i={\rm d,nd}.

(282)

Then by formulae listed in Sec.
F.4
, we obtain:

Lemma F.2
.

Given
g^{(i)}=e^{i\vec{p}^{(i)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}e^{i\vec{\theta}^{(i)}\cdot\vec{\tau}}
, we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{x^{(j)}_{k}}E_{a;0}^{\rm d})(e^{iw\tau_{3}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})

\displaystyle=0,\ j=1,2,\text{ and }k=1,2

(283)

\displaystyle(\partial_{x^{(j)}_{3}}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0})(e^{iw\tau_{3}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})

\displaystyle=0,\ j=1,2

for all
w=p-i\theta\in\mathbb{C}
, where
x^{(i)}_{j}
denotes
p^{(i)}_{j}
or
\theta^{(i)}_{j}
. Moreover, consider the matrix
A
and
B
in Eq. (
266
).
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}
satisfies that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})A^{-1}B

\displaystyle=0=\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})A^{-1}B^{T},

(284)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})A^{-1}B^{T}

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}}),

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})A^{-1}B

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}}),

\displaystyle B^{T}A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})

\displaystyle=0=BA^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}}),

\displaystyle BA^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}}),

\displaystyle B^{T}A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}(e^{iw\tau_{k}},e^{iw\tau_{3}}),

where
\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(i)}}=(\partial_{\vec{p}_{1}^{(i)}},\partial_{\vec{p}_{2}^{(i)}},\partial_{\vec{p}_{3}^{(i)}},\partial_{\vec{\theta}_{1}^{(i)}},\partial_{\vec{\theta}_{2}^{(i)}},\partial_{\vec{\theta}_{3}^{(i)}})^{T}
and
\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(i)}}^{T}
is its transpose.

The second lemma is on the inverse of the Hessian matrix (
266
). Recalling Eq. (
268
), one has that the inverse of
f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
, denoted as
H_{k,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}
, is

H_{k,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A^{-1}+A^{-1}\tilde{B}(D-\tilde{C}A^{-1}\tilde{B})^{-1}\tilde{C}A^{-1}&-A^{-1}\tilde{B}(D-\tilde{C}A^{-1}\tilde{B})^{-1}\\
-(D-\tilde{C}A^{-1}\tilde{B})^{-1}\tilde{C}A^{-1}&(D-\tilde{C}A^{-1}\tilde{B})^{-1}\end{array}\right).

(285)

Since
\tilde{C}A^{-1}\tilde{B}=0
and
D=f^{\prime\prime}_{k-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
, we have

H_{k,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A^{-1}+A^{-1}\tilde{B}H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\tilde{C}A^{-1}&-A^{-1}\tilde{B}H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\\
-H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\tilde{C}A^{-1}&H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\end{array}\right).

(286)

For
k=1
,
H_{1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}=A^{-1}
. Thus one has that
\tilde{B}H_{1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\tilde{C}=0
and

H_{2,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A^{-1}&-A^{-1}\tilde{B}H_{1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\\
-H_{1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\tilde{C}A^{-1}&H_{1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\end{array}\right).

Doing this successively,
one has
\tilde{B}H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\tilde{C}=0
,
and

H_{k,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A^{-1}&-A^{-1}\tilde{B}H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\\
-H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\tilde{C}A^{-1}&H_{k-1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}\end{array}\right).

(287)

Finally,
H_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}
can be obtained with this recurrence relation and the initial data
H_{1,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}=A^{-1}
. The result is as follows:

Lemma F.3
.

H_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}
satisfies that

(H_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{mn}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}(-1)^{|m-n|}A^{-1}(BA^{-1})^{|m-n|},&m<n\\
A^{-1},&m=n\\
(-1)^{|m-n|}A^{-1}(B^{T}A^{-1})^{|m-n|},&m>n\\
\end{array}\right.

(288)

where
H_{k,\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1}
is arranged as a block matrix as
f^{\prime\prime}_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}
in (
266
)], with
(H_{k;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{mn}
as a block.

Now the theorem (
IV.2
) can be proven.

Proof of Theorem
IV.2
.

For convenience, we define
s_{o}=M_{+}+N_{+}
.
By Theorem
IV.1
,
\langle\mathcal{M}\rangle_{z_{e}}
is of order
t^{s_{o}}
or higher.
Adopting the result from equation (
277
),
O(t^{s_{o}})
only occurs when
s=s_{o}
and
\mathfrak{D}^{(s_{o})}
is of
O(t^{0})
.

Set
s=s_{o}
in the definition (
278
) of
\mathfrak{D}^{(s)}
.
For given
l
and
j
, if there is one
E^{(m)}
taking the form of
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}+O(t)
not being acted by derivatives, then the eventual evaluation at the critical point will vanish.
Moreover, in
\mathfrak{D}^{(s_{o})}
, on one hand
it contains
2s_{o}
of
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}+O(t)
,
and on the other hand the maximum order of derivative that can act on
\prod E^{(i)}
is
2s_{o}
, which only occurs when
l=s_{o}
.
Therefore,
only when
l=s_{o}
and
m=0
, all
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}+O(t)
are acted by derivatives.
Finally,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{(s_{o})}=\left(\frac{\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta}{2})}{\theta^{2}}\right)^{|\mathcal{M}|-1}\frac{2^{-s_{o}}}{s_{o}!}\left[\sum_{a,b=1}^{6(|\mathcal{M}|-1)}H^{-1}_{ab}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{a}\partial x_{b}}\right]^{s_{o}}\prod_{i=1}^{|\mathcal{M}|-1}E^{(i)}\Bigg|_{g^{(l)}=e^{iw\tau_{3}},\forall l},

(289)

where
|\mathcal{M}|
denotes the number of factors in the monomial
\mathcal{M}
.

To calculate (
289
), we employ the notion introduced in Lemma
F.2
and
F.3
to treat
H^{-1}
as a block matrix. Then
\sum_{a,b=1}^{6(|\mathcal{M}|-1)}H^{-1}_{ab}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{a}\partial x_{b}}
is rewritten as

\sum_{a,b=1}^{6(|\mathcal{M}|-1)}H^{-1}_{ab}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{a}\partial x_{b}}=\sum_{m,n=1}^{|\mathcal{M}|-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}\left(\hat{H}^{-1}_{|\mathcal{M}|-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\right)_{mn}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}}.

(290)

We then expand
\left[\sum_{m,n=1}^{|\mathcal{M}|-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}\left(\hat{H}^{-1}_{|\mathcal{M}|-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\right)_{mn}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}}\right]^{s_{o}}
and let each individual term of the expansion act on
\prod_{i=1}^{|\mathcal{M}|-1}E^{(i)}
.
In each individual term of the expansion, it contains derivative with respect to certain
\vec{x}^{(q)}
. Because each
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}+O(t)
only depends on certain
\vec{x}^{(q)}
,
we only consider the case when all derivatives are paired with all
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}+O(t)
with the same argument. For the other terms of the expansion, they give vanishing results because of the evaluation at the critical point.

The procedure mentioned above is equivalent to the follows. We first partition these
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}+O(t)
into ordering pairs. Denote all possibilities of the partition as
\mathcal{P}
. Given a pair
(E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})+O(t),E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})+O(t))
in a partition
p\in\mathcal{P}
. It can be acted by

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}(H_{\mathcal{M}-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{mn}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}},

(291)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m-1)}}^{T}(H_{\mathcal{M}-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{m-1,n}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}},

(292)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}(H_{\mathcal{M}-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{m,n-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}},

(293)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m-1)}}^{T}(H_{\mathcal{M}-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{m-1,n-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}}

(294)

According to Lemmas
F.3
and
F.2
,

(1)

If
m<n
, only the operator (
293
) gives non-vanishing results which reads

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})(H_{\mathcal{M}-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{m,n-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})

(295)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})

(2)

If
m>n
, only the operator (
292
) gives non-vanishing results which reads

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m-1)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})(H_{\mathcal{M}-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}^{-1})_{m-1,n}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})

(296)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m-1)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n}}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m-1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)}),

where in the last step we used
A=A^{T}
.

It should be reminded that an evaluation at the critical point has been done in Eqs.(
295
) and (
296
).

According to the results in Eqs. (
295
) and (
296
), rather than partitioning the
E^{\rm nd}_{a;0}+O(t)
into
ordering
pairs, we can identify the partitions
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
if
p_{1}
can be the same as
p_{2}
by reordering each of its pairs. The set with this identification will be denoted by
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
. Then we finally have

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{m,n=1}^{|\mathcal{M}|-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}\left(\hat{H}^{-1}_{|\mathcal{M}|-1;\vec{p}_{o},\vec{\theta}_{o}}\right)_{mn}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n)}}\right]^{s_{o}}\prod_{i=1}^{|\mathcal{M}|-1}E^{(i)}\Bigg|_{g^{(l)}=e^{iw\tau_{3}},\forall l}

(297)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{s_{o}}\sum_{p\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m,n)\in p\\
\text{with }m<n\end{subarray}}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})\prod_{l\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{C}}}E^{\rm d}_{a_{l};0}(e^{iw\tau_{3}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})+O(t)

where
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{C}}
denotes the set of those
E^{(i)}
of the form
E^{\rm d}_{a;0}
. Substituting the results, we finally have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\psi_{z_{e}}|\mathcal{M}|\psi_{z_{e}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{z_{e}}|\psi_{z_{e}}\rangle}=

\displaystyle\left(\left(2t\right)^{s_{o}}\frac{1}{s_{o}!}\sum_{p\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m,n)\in p\\
\text{with }m<n\end{subarray}}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})\right)\times

(298)

\displaystyle\prod_{l\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{C}}}E^{\rm d}_{a_{l};0}(e^{iw\tau_{3}},e^{iw\tau_{3}})+O(t^{s_{o}+1})

Note that in

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m,n)\in p\\
\text{with }m<n\end{subarray}}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)}),

the first argument in the most left factor and the second argument in the most right factor cannot be acted by the derivative operator. Therefore, by the similar discussion as above, one verifies that

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\psi_{z_{e}}|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|\psi_{z_{e}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{z_{e}}|\psi_{z_{e}}\rangle}=

\displaystyle\left(\left(2t\right)^{s_{o}}\frac{1}{s_{o}!}\sum_{p\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m,n)\in p\\
\text{with }m<n\end{subarray}}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(m)}}^{T}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{m};0}(\vec{x}^{(m-1)},\vec{x}^{(m)})A^{-1}\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(n-1)}}E^{\rm nd}_{a_{n};0}(\vec{x}^{(n-1)},\vec{x}^{(n)})\right)+O(t^{s_{o}+1}).

(299)

Moreover,
E^{\rm d}_{a_{l};(0)}(e^{i\omega\tau_{3}},e^{i\omega\tau_{3}})
represents the leading order of the expectation value of its corresponding operator. Since these
E^{\rm d}_{a_{l};(0)}(e^{i\omega\tau_{3}},e^{i\omega\tau_{3}})
do not vanish, we conclude that
\langle\mathcal{M}\rangle_{z_{e}}
is of order
t^{s_{o}}
if and only if
\langle\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\rangle_{z_{e}}
is. Thus Eq. (
65
) is true for the case when both sides are of order
t^{s_{o}}
.
Moreover, both sides of Eq. (
65
) are of
O(t^{M_{+}+N_{+}})
or higher, and if the leading order terms of both sides are not
O(t^{M_{+}+N_{+}})
, then they both vanish at
O(t^{M_{+}+N_{+}})
.

∎

F.4
derivative of the matrix element of
p^{i}(e)
and
D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{ab}(h_{e})

Note that the results show below ignore the terms of order
t
and higher. We denote
\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(i)}}:=\left(\nabla_{\vec{p}^{(i)}},\nabla_{\vec{\theta}^{(i)}}\right)
and
x^{*}
is the complex conjugate of
x
.

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{1}_{s}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}i\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right),0,-\frac{ip\sin(\theta)\text{csch}(p)}{2\theta},\frac{ip\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\text{csch}(p)}{\theta},0\right)^{T}

(300)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{1}_{s}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{2}_{s}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(-\frac{1}{2}i\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right),-\frac{1}{2},0,-\frac{ip\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\text{csch}(p)}{\theta},-\frac{ip\sin(\theta)\text{csch}(p)}{2\theta},0\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{2}_{s}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{3}_{s}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(0,0,-\frac{1}{2},0,0,-\frac{i}{2}\right)^{T}=\left(\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{3}_{s}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}\right)^{*}.

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{1}_{t}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\Bigg(\frac{e^{i\theta}+e^{p-i\theta}}{2e^{p}+2},\frac{ie^{-i\theta}\left(e^{p}-e^{2i\theta}\right)}{2\left(e^{p}+1\right)},0,\frac{e^{-i\theta}\left(-1+e^{i\theta}\right)p\left(e^{2p}+e^{i\theta}\right)}{2\theta\left(e^{2p}-1\right)},

(301)

\displaystyle\frac{ie^{-i\theta}\left(-1+e^{i\theta}\right)p\left(e^{2p}-e^{i\theta}\right)}{2\theta\left(e^{2p}-1\right)},0\Bigg)^{T}=\left(\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{1}_{t}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{2}_{t}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\Bigg(\frac{ie^{-i\theta}\left(-e^{p}+e^{2i\theta}\right)}{2\left(e^{p}+1\right)},\frac{e^{i\theta}+e^{p-i\theta}}{2e^{p}+2},0,\frac{ie^{-i\theta}\left(-1+e^{i\theta}\right)p\left(-e^{2p}+e^{i\theta}\right)}{2\theta\left(e^{2p}-1\right)},

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-i\theta}\left(-1+e^{i\theta}\right)p\left(e^{2p}+e^{i\theta}\right)}{2\theta\left(e^{2p}-1\right)},0\Bigg)^{T}=\left(\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{2}_{t}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{3}_{t}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(0,0,\frac{1}{2},0,0,\frac{i}{2}\right)^{T}=\left(\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\hat{p}^{3}_{t}(e)|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}\right)^{*}.

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(0,0,-\frac{1}{4}e^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}},0,0,-\frac{1}{4}ie^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}}\right)^{T},

(302)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(-\frac{e^{\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},\frac{ie^{\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},0,-\frac{i\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},-\frac{\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},0\right)^{T},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(-\frac{e^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},-\frac{ie^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},0,-\frac{ie^{p}\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},\frac{e^{p}\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},0\right)^{T},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(1)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(0,0,\frac{1}{4}e^{\frac{i\theta}{2}},0,0,\frac{1}{4}ie^{\frac{i\theta}{2}}\right)^{T},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(0,0,\frac{1}{4}e^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}},0,0,-\frac{1}{4}ie^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}}\right)^{T},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(\frac{e^{\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},-\frac{ie^{\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},0,-\frac{ie^{p}\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},-\frac{e^{p}\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},0\right)^{T},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},\frac{ie^{-\frac{i\theta}{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)}{2p},0,-\frac{i\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},\frac{\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\theta+\theta e^{p}},0\right)^{T},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{x}^{(2)}}\frac{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|D^{\frac{1}{2}}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}(h_{e})|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}{\langle\psi_{g^{(1)}}|\psi_{g^{(2)}}\rangle}=\left(0,0,-\frac{1}{4}e^{\frac{i\theta}{2}},0,0,\frac{1}{4}ie^{\frac{i\theta}{2}}\right)^{T}.
